# UC Agents Roadmap — Canonical Plan

**Audience:** Every agent contributing to this repo (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini, future bridges). This is the single doc you consult before starting work on the agent runtime. Keep it in sync.

**Last synced:** 2026-08-17

**Why this doc exists:** Two agents (Claude Code + Codex) have been independently converging on the same Hermes-style architecture for OpenSwan. Each shipped complementary pieces without full awareness of the other. This doc reconciles both into one plan so we stop building parallel stacks.

## 1. North star

OpenSwan should behave like an agent that **improves over time** and **can help with any task**:

- Typed tool-use loop on any chat-completion model (Claude default, Qwen/Ollama for local).
- Rich tool registry (file ops, verification, browser, memory, skill management, subagent delegation).
- Procedural memory as skills ([agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) SKILL.md format so users can bring Claude Code / Cursor / Codex skills straight in).
- Declarative memory scoped per circle + per user.
- Post-hoc quality scoring + regression benchmarks today; offline self-evolution (DSPy/GEPA style) once we have ≥50 skills + ≥1K runs.
- HITL gates on destructive writes to memory or the skill library. Appends
  (`save_memory`, `user_memory.manage` append) go through immediately;
  `replace`/`delete` file an approval with a diff. This is the shipped rule
  (see §2 "User memory agent tool"); the line previously read "every write",
  which no code path has ever implemented and which read as a stronger
  guarantee than the product makes.

Operators use the reviewed consolidated SQL rather than renaming historical migrations during deployment. The optional fixed-loopback bridge probe performs only unauthenticated `GET /desktop/health`; it never invokes refresh or restarts the bridge. An exact v2 direct supervisor (`dev-stack-keepalive` or `start-dev`) may refresh only after the bridge proves complete session/work/browser/private-capability idleness, validates the fixed five-file source manifest, and receives an authenticated reservation. The supervisor snapshots those exact validated bytes into private 0700/0600 storage, the old bridge flushes the 202 response, rechecks safety, commits the reservation, and exits 75; replacement runs from that immutable snapshot through the original filenames, then must return signed post-listen ONLINE plus exact instance, manifest, and snapshot lineage through `/desktop/health`. Bounded retries retain the same snapshot, preserve ordinary crash history, and clean it after ready, rejection, exhaustion, or shutdown. Unsupported, legacy, foreign, ambiguous, unhealthy, changing, or non-idle supervisors remain typed-409 fail closed and never reserve, drain, or exit. Live catalog and authenticated two-user read canaries are separate, require both explicit CLI switches and exact `RUN_LIVE_*` confirmations plus an exact HTTPS project identity, never print fixture/credential values, and contain no SQL-apply or mutating HTTP path. The live catalog additionally requires absolute reviewed non-symlink paths for both `psql` and the CA bundle, then pins a minimal libpq environment to `verify-full`, preventing caller executable lookup, inherited `PG*` overrides, or plaintext fallback. | **Office dashboard truth, durable layout, complete floor presets, private preferences, and cost semantics** | `src/lib/{officeDashboardPersistence,officeLayoutLocalCache,officeLayoutSaveQueueCore,officeLayoutSaveReceiptCore,officePreferenceWriteQueueCore,officeFloorPresetCore}.ts` + `src/lib/chatAttentionQueue.ts` + `src/lib/runHistoryFilterCore.ts` + `src/lib/officeAgents.ts` + `src/lib/{agentIdentity,agentPresence,agentHeartbeat,sessionCache,sessionTags}.ts` + `src/lib/claudeUsage.ts` + `src/components/chat/RunHistoryDrawer.tsx` + `src/components/office/OfficeOpsBoardCards.tsx` + `src/screens/circles/tabs/OfficeTab.tsx` + `src/screens/circles/tabs/office/{OfficeSections,AgentRunsPanel,AgentActivityPanel,Whiteboard}.tsx` + `supabase/migrations/{20260811120000_office_dashboard_state_and_floor_presets,20260813140000_office_layout_exact_save_receipt,20260813220000_office_user_preferences,20260817130000_agent_identity_primary_rpc}.sql` + `docs/RUN_THIS_SQL.sql` §§37/45/47 + `scripts/{office-dashboard-state,office-cost-stability,office-layout-local-cache,office-layout-save-queue-core,office-layout-save-receipt-core,office-preference-write-queue-core,office-session-storage-scope,circle-office-exact-auth-scope,office-presence-heartbeat-authority,office-private-runtime-wiring,agent-identity-exact-authority,agent-identity-cross-realm-cache-ordering,agent-identity-primary-rpc-sql,office-user-preferences-sql,office-user-preferences-sql-parity,run-history-drawer-exact-cancel}-smoketest.ts` + `scripts/{office-dashboard-sql,office-user-preferences-sql-behavior,agent-identity-primary-rpc-sql-behavior}-smoketest.sh` | Owns truthful Office run visibility, spend presentation, and private per-circle workspace state. Only fresh `planning` or `running` rows qualify as Active; queued, paused, and approval-waiting rows belong to Other, and processing rows quiet beyond the shared 30-minute freshness window become `STALE · NOT ACTIVE`. Opening Run History is strictly presentation-only and never reaps or terminalizes a row. Every list, direct-focus, step, artifact, child-run, and Realtime history read is bound to one exact captured user/circle/token/generation; retired snapshots are synchronously ineligible for render and read failures never pose as verified empty. A stale row may be closed only through the explicit confirmed Cancelled action under that exact authority; the guarded row/circle/owner/status/heartbeat compare-and-set requires one exact returned receipt, visibly reconciles a zero-row conflict, and never fabricates completion or replays after authority retirement. The Office Mine lane uses only exact owned DB/provider-main ids and provenance-stamped local connection ids, never display-name equivalence. Daily, cumulative-session, and lifetime agent costs are separate: one exact durable Office row owns daily/lifetime totals, local cache and identity history cannot overwrite daily cost, ambiguous provider attribution fails closed, and rolling 24h/7d/30d spend comes from durable server telemetry. Transient usage-read failure retains the last server snapshot rather than displaying a false zero. The main dashboard intentionally omits the redundant running-cost/reset strip and personal presence/timer picker; durable spend remains available in its reporting surfaces. Attention dismissal is revision-keyed and durable per user/circle, and old paused/waiting rows age out rather than resurfacing forever. Office floor layout writes are sanitized, user-and-circle-scoped, serialized, and monotonically versioned; the local envelope is written and read back atomically before server dispatch. The queue retains only the newest unsaved snapshot, re-drains after a busy writer, rejects snapshots stale against the active write, retains rejected operations for retry, and prevents an older failure or conflict from erasing a newer edit. Local-cache and server operations have bounded caller deadlines; an unabortable operation quarantines only its exact user/circle lane until it settles, while unrelated scopes continue independently. Server success requires an exact accepted-version receipt that is still the newest local generation and mutation epoch. Retry refreshes the exact pending credential, while optional private preference enrichment cannot downgrade a successful authoritative layout read. Primary-agent selection uses the owner-bound transactional §47 RPC plus a partial unique index, returning a bounded exact provider-row receipt before generation-fenced publication. Every verified exact identity mutation then takes one bounded exact-scope Web Lock on web, rereads a count-complete owner snapshot with the captured bearer and an abort signal, and replaces the cache only from that server truth; an older tab completion therefore cannot republish its stale receipt after a newer tab. Web lock absence/timeout preserves `serverSaved: true` and fails local publication closed, native retains the in-process queue, and no client timestamp is ordering authority. Only a clean verified success whose guarded terminal write was observed may be reported complete or enter reusable/archive success-memory, proof, XP, or dependent-agent paths; cancellation stays cancelled, while cap/guard/edge stops, unresolved delegated work, failed/blocked/manual/unverified required proof, or an unverified final database write retain their honest recovery state. Every planned child must return an explicitly completed summary/result for delegation to count as complete. For one bounded two- or three-action Chat or Room contract, `openSwanMultiActionCompletionCore.ts` validates exact A1-A3 ledger shape and grouped dependencies, exactly one report event, unique earlier runtime-owned tool-use evidence, operation-specific completion tools, authoritative mutation and target binding, status agreement, and sequential evidence order without reading provider prose. A derived text action may use one deferred `run.publish_action_artifact` claim bound to its exact A-id and allowlisted kind, but only an exact successful canonical `agent_run_artifacts` insert returned before the singleton final report becomes evidence. The reporter must cite that earlier publisher tool-use id. A pure draft may complete from that durable artifact, while a source-grounded summary, analysis, comparison, or recommendation requires both the exact supporting read evidence and the durable artifact. For grounded output the cited read must also come from a strictly earlier provider iteration than publication, so a same-round read and artifact cannot pretend the model observed the source result. An explicit file, SaaS, app, browser, desktop, or other external destination remains mutation-bound; publication never substitutes for its operation-specific mutation proof. The bounded redacted content stays in the canonical artifact row while the evidence ledger and Chat/Room A# snapshots remain value-free. Chat persists only an opaque `canonicalArtifactId` plus a truncated inline display copy, then batch rehydrates only a row whose artifact id, run, circle, title, A-id, and artifact kind all match exactly; completion never trusts the message copy. Unmapped reads, unsupported mutations, and assistant-authored deliverables without the required read and/or durable typed receipt are `evidenceUnavailable`, never inferred complete from nearby evidence. Missing, pending, blocked, invalid, or unavailable coverage becomes partial `action_coverage_incomplete`; an explicitly failed action becomes `action_coverage_failed`; only an all-completed, semantically bound ledger may be verified. The runtime carries provider `toolUseId` and one-based provider iteration through both typed and legacy adapters, forces contract tool dispatch to respect dependency order, and persists only a bounded value-free action summary. Per-run metadata merges are serialized, optimistic-CAS retried, exact-row returned, and recursively verified so a stage write cannot erase or falsely acknowledge the action snapshot; a missing run or unverified merge becomes `persistence_unverified`. Room messages follow their immutable RLS contract: local UI owns transient typing and each clarification, runtime failure, or final response performs exactly one checked INSERT, never a placeholder UPDATE. Room reload queries the newest bounded page by descending timestamp plus id, then reverses it for chronological display, so rooms beyond 200 messages do not get stuck on their oldest rows. Cross-surface metadata keeps only bounded terminal scalars, never checkpoint contents. A resumable Chat message may persist a value-free locator bound to the exact circle, user, thread, run, source message, and device-local transcript event. Continue synchronously claims that locator once, resolves only that checkpoint before any new transcript write or model/tool work, and never falls back to a latest-checkpoint scan when the explicit locator is stale, mismatched, missing, invalid, or superseded. Browser-backed plans that are merely approval-ready remain deferred/waiting approval rather than completed. The shared `agentExecutionCore.runAgent` path remains default-on; `uc_openswan_typed_core=0` is a manual next-turn rollback lever and never an automatic mid-run fallback. | Source/typecheck/focused-smoke current 2026-08-12; live provider, authenticated cross-surface refresh, cross-process resume, concurrent Supabase cancellation, and deployment proof pending |

Every ordinary attachment-dependent Chat model turn is bound to one authoritative A1 or bounded A1-A3 OpenSwan contract. Before any provider, vision, connected-agent, desktop, or other attachment consumer runs, Chat observes the exact persisted user-message id, compare-and-set links every exact staged database attachment to that row with returned-row verification, hashes exact bytes, and assembles one sealed private turn source outside public run/message metadata. Exact loaded human-member identity wins every agent-name alias; a human-addressed upload persists and links, then performs zero AI dispatch. Exact selected connected-agent ownership is preserved, but a non-image private file blocks rather than silently falling through to another executor. A saved Continue with pending files, attachment-bearing slash shortcut, unsafe native/picker-only value, incomplete upload, scope drift, or failed linkage exits before consumer I/O and leaves the files available for a safe retry. Plan mode retains the bound contract and exact persisted source-message id without executing it. The provider sees only the value-free projection and may inspect content only through deferred read-only `attachments.read_source({ attachmentId })`; it accepts no URL, storage key, path, base64, or raw byte authority. The exact unmodified user request travels separately from every augmented runtime prompt; attachment-derived network or external access has no authority when that original text is absent, and the public HTTP boundary blocks loopback, private, link-local, credential-bearing, unsafe-redirect, and attachment-injected destinations. The runtime releases only bounded inline extraction or sanitized visual observation as untrusted data, hides it from tool-event persistence, and mints target-bound action evidence only after the runtime-issued receipt resolves against the exact manifest/source/content identity. Missing persistence, thread drift, ambiguous visual/file identity, unreadable content, receipt/hash mismatch, or filename/prose-only claims stop the whole contract before or without verified completion. §39 makes attachment linkage a database-enforced one-way exact owner/circle/thread/user-message transition while preserving bounded owner OCR updates. §40 makes staged metadata owner-only, makes linked metadata and private Storage objects follow exact live private/shared/circle thread visibility, binds every admitted row to one unique exact owner path/object, denies authenticated Storage update and every anon operation, and retains trusted service maintenance behind restrictive guards resilient to permissive policy drift. §41 adds restrictive authenticated SELECT and UPDATE guards so only the exact requester may read, resolve, or consume canonical schema-v2 `desktop.open_attachment` approvals, preventing a circle peer from learning their attachment/message/hash binding payload; the UPDATE boundary is anchored to the old row, and PostgreSQL/service-role maintenance remains available. The legacy path-bearing desktop branch remains disabled. One explicit open/load/preview/show request for one safely typed, durably linked upload may now enter `desktop.open_attachment({ attachmentId })`: Chat seals exact bytes, stages them behind a branded one-shot process-private capability, requires one non-batchable approval, resumes only by synchronously claiming the exact in-memory lease, dispatches once through the durable action ledger, and accepts completion only from a runtime-issued receipt plus fresh frontmost-app evidence. The bridge uses private 0700/0600 staging, exact file dev/inode/realpath/stat/hash, a production-pinned `/usr/bin/open`, root-owned non-writable signed application bundles, private exact process/window/AXDocument/path/open-inode proof, idle TTL deletion, guarded stale-instance cleanup after crashes, no shell, and no public path/bearer. Any edit/save/export/transform, copied or expired capability, wrong scope/hash/message/ledger, missing proof, or post-dispatch ambiguity remains blocked or `outcome_unknown` with no replay. | Source/typecheck/focused-smoke current 2026-08-13; §§39-41 application plus live upload/provider/private-thread/native-app canary proof pending |

The default edge continuation now consults the current canonical approval policy: auto/read calls do not invoke a merely-present review callback, ask calls either reach one exact surface review or a named handler-owned durable boundary, missing/unknown policy fails closed, and only calls that can actually reach the surface callback serialize the batch. WordPress writes and the generic risk floor use the same schema-v2 approval machinery: SHA-256 binds exact actor/scope/run/tool-use/iteration/arguments, audit rows keep only safe digests, and one `applied_at`/dispatch-binding compare-and-set authorizes one handler entry. Continuations bind opaque identity/version/nonce plus one client-generated dispatch claim. Phase one compare-and-sets `pending` / `client_pending` to `dispatch_claimed` / `client_dispatching` before any local execution; phase two stores results and compare-and-sets to `results_claimed` / `client_resuming` before model resume. The exact transcript is held transiently and durably sealed with AES-256-GCM under the dedicated `SWANBOT_CONTINUATION_ENCRYPTION_SECRET`; additional authenticated data binds canonical exact run/user/circle row identity while the public envelope remains strict, bounded, value-free, and identifier-free, so cross-row ciphertext transplantation fails closed. Missing key material withholds client-only tools and blocks resume, unreadable or legacy checkpoints close without replay, and unapplied §29 adds service-only expiry/unsafe-state cleanup plus terminal checkpoint scrubbing and rejects authenticated cloning, mutation, or deletion of protected active v2 rows while preserving reads and service-role/Postgres maintenance. Only an exact retry of the winning dispatch claim is idempotently acknowledged. Competing, mixed-version/state, ambiguous, expired, or lost claim-bound paths seal `outcome_unknown` and never replay. Edge-side memory/task/mission/message/room/approval writers latch before handler entry; any later provider/runtime ambiguity becomes a non-retryable `server_mutation_outcome_unknown`, and the client recovers the structured non-2xx body before the v1 fallback boundary so a completed write cannot be replayed through another lane. Readiness ignores all three active stop reasons. Durable tool event summaries are key-free and value-free. Source-derived catalog parity is pinned at **25 server-side + 59 client-delegated = 84 total**. The bounded semantic `browser.wait_for` and `browser.scroll` lanes use the same canonical client runtime. The `browser.locator_actionability` lane is read-only advisory evidence for one fresh exact browser target; it does not authorize or bind a later mutation. This is not a universal guarantee for future tools/callers. The device-local loop remains opt-in/default-off. Handler entry atomically records `dispatched`; duplicate or terminal calls never re-enter the app handler. Because concurrent claimers may receive the same claim token, a worker that loses before handler entry leaves the claim unfinalized/reclaimable instead of overwriting another worker's dispatched action; any error after confirmed start can finish only `outcome_unknown`. Direct table writes are revoked and SECURITY DEFINER RPCs enforce exact identity. The TypeScript parser independently rejects forged failed-after-dispatch payloads. | Source, ledger/runtime/database-authority smokes current through 2026-08-05; live application/contention status remains owned by the SQL checklist below |

That key survives refresh-sensitive long-tail app canonicalization, while the separate exact program fingerprint still rejects drift; a terminal duplicate/refresh cannot enter the adapter again. `open_app` selects exactly one branch from fresh initial state: launch plus bounded wait when stopped, or focus when already running in the background, never both. Explicit focus never launches. Fresh exact running/frontmost proof is required; ambiguity becomes verification-only/no-replay. Generic recovery pauses in verification-only mode after a later foreground change and requires explicit resume instead of automatic refocus/relaunch; durable foreground-lease wiring remains pending. Semantic reads remain model-assisted, and document/UI mutations keep normal approvals/evidence. | Source/smoke/typecheck current 2026-08-10 with §26 catalog present; no updated live lifecycle GUI, duplicate-refresh, bridge-restart, foreground-override, or competing-client contention proof |
| **Exact Chat plan approval continuity** | `src/lib/exactPlanApprovalContinuityCore.ts` + `src/lib/computerTaskState.ts` + `src/lib/computerTaskStateModel.ts` + `src/screens/circles/tabs/ChatTab.tsx` + `scripts/exact-program-authority-smoketest.ts` + `scripts/chat-lane-outcome-smoketest.ts` + `scripts/computer-task-runtime-context-smoketest.ts` + `scripts/computer-task-state-provider-smoketest.ts` | Owns refresh-safe continuation for a compiler-owned exact plan approval. The local task state stores only a bounded, credential-free correlation bound to the exact approval id, authenticated requester/circle/thread, session/action/expiry, immutable program, original request, and approval-intent fingerprints. Hydration and Realtime notifications always perform an exact authenticated row requery; event payloads are never authority. Approved rows resume the original request through the normal re-fingerprinting and one-shot database consume gate. The durable pointer remains until that gate wins its compare-and-set, then is cleared in the handler before app/browser/file dispatch. Rejected, expired, consumed, duplicate, stale, legacy, or mismatched rows terminalize without replay. | Source/smoke verified 2026-08-06; full Chat-tab refresh covered, live cross-client/database contention pending |

Provider-catalog deployment compatibility was hardened 2026-08-14. Before
`list_models` fan-out, the client probes the public `llm-proxy` GET capability
list once; an older healthy deployment that does not advertise that action
uses the short-cache curated fallback without one expected HTTP 400 per
connected provider. Read-only live inspection found deployed `llm-proxy` v21
healthy but not yet advertising `list_models`; deploying the source Edge
capability remains a separate gate.

Chat startup credential/auth resilience was hardened 2026-08-14. Background
memory embedding now metadata-preflights the active OpenAI key and persists a
24-hour block against the exact `(key id, updated_at)` version after
`credential_unreadable`; a Marketplace key write clears that block and re-arms
the orphan sweep immediately. The retry scheduler is separate from the live
embedding drain timer. App auth bootstrap also passes its already-validated
user/token scope into local-agent auto-connect, and Claude/Codex/Gemini/Cursor
publication/status writes bind that bearer directly instead of reacquiring the
mutable browser Auth Web Lock on each poll. Expected lifecycle aborts remain
retryable and quiet; other publication errors remain visible.

Deployment wording in the SwanBot v2 ownership row refers to the verified
2026-08-05 production snapshot. That row—not generic Run persistence—owns the
new source-only complete-summary invariant across pending, checkpoint failure,
continuation close/seal, cancel, failure, and terminal writers. Bounded
operation/code-only diagnostics are currently guaranteed for those hardened
summary/checkpoint/close/CAS writes, not every legacy `agent_runs` or event-log
call in the Edge function. The updated Edge source still requires redeployment
and fresh cohorts before any newer production-readiness or M4-completion claim.

Current v2 source also binds service-role `messages.create` to the authenticated
active Chat thread. Fresh requests authorize that exact user/circle/thread,
encrypted continuations retain it for resume, and missing or model-mismatched
thread arguments fail before insert. The insert takes `thread_id` only from the
authorized context, closing the source-level `messages?select=id` 400/misroute
seam. Deployment and live §31 behavioral proof are still pending.

### Duplicate / deprecated files (keep but do not extend)

| File | Why still here | Replacement |
|---|---|---|
| `src/lib/openswanRuntimeToolLoop.ts` | No live dispatch caller remains. `openswanSessionRuntime.ts` imports only `extractBrowserPlansFromToolActions`; the old loop export is rollback-era code. | Move the extraction helper to its canonical consumer, then delete the retired loop after the rollback window. |
| `swanbot-ai/index.ts`'s in-process tool loop (2904 L) | Live edge function; users rely on it. | Phase 1c rewrite — thin wrapper around `AgentExecutionCore`. Deploy as `swanbot-v2-ai` first to allow rollback. |

---

## 3. Phase status

### Phase 0 — Production safety (shipped)

#### 2026-08-12 OpenSwan terminal outcome truth

- `openswanSessionRuntimeAdapters.ts` now defines the single terminal receipt
  for an OpenSwan turn: `succeeded`, `partial`, `failed`, or `cancelled`, plus
  the bounded stop reason, `completionVerified`, `resumable`, and optional
  checkpoint. Provider prose is not an input to this classification.
- `openswanSessionRuntime.ts` uses that same receipt for the returned turn,
  transcript, run metadata, eval projection, and final run write. Only a clean
  verified success calls the completion writer and enters reusable/archive
  success-memory paths. A user stop remains cancelled; cap, runtime-guard, and
  edge-failure turns remain non-complete and preserve partial work/checkpoints.
  Explicit failed verification becomes `verification_failed`; blocked or
  manual-required checks become `verification_blocked`; and a present coding
  receipt with edits but no passing checks becomes `verification_unverified`.
  A missing coding receipt for an ordinary read-only/Q&A turn does not by
  itself block clean completion. The guarded final writer returns an applied,
  cancelled, or unverified disposition; the receipt is rebuilt afterward so a
  concurrent cancellation wins and an unobserved write becomes
  `persistence_unverified` rather than a reported success.
- Delegation starts incomplete and becomes complete only when every planned
  subagent returns a result whose typed parent summary and summary metadata both
  report completion. Missing or non-complete child results produce
  `delegation_incomplete`; the parent model's prose cannot hide that partial
  terminal. Transcript and child-ledger step labels use each child's actual
  typed status instead of unconditional `completed` copy.
  The current database enum has no partial state, so non-cancelled incomplete
  runs close as failed rather than lingering as active.
- Ordinary one-action turns now apply the same runtime-owned truth rule to
  tools. A failed planner-high required call, or any failed mutation-policy
  call, closes the turn as failed with `action_coverage_failed`; a blocked,
  manual-required, pending, or unknown required call closes partial with
  `action_coverage_incomplete`. Optional exploratory read failures remain
  non-gating. Both legacy `tool/status` and typed `tool_name/status` event
  shapes feed the same pure disposition, so optimistic provider prose cannot
  turn an attempted required action into completion outside the A1-A3 path.
- `ChatTab` projects OpenSwan lane health and persisted message outcome from
  the typed receipt through `chatLaneOutcome.ts` and `chatOutcomeSignals.ts`.
  Partial, failed, and cancelled replies lead with truthful status/recovery
  copy even when the provider response sounds successful, and a returned stop
  never auto-launches a connected repair agent. Resumable stops expose a
  bounded Continue path only when the originating message carries an exact
  value-free `OpenSwanResumeLocator`. The locator binds circle, user, thread,
  run, source-message, and device-local transcript-event identity without
  persisting checkpoint contents. Chat claims it synchronously once; the
  runtime resolves that exact event before a new transcript write or any
  provider/tool work. A missing, stale, mismatched, malformed, or superseded
  locator stops without falling back to the latest checkpoint or replaying the
  task.
- Room Chat persists the same bounded terminal state/reason/verification and
  leads non-success messages with deterministic status copy. Mission completion
  treats the receipt as authoritative over optimistic prose. Feed/Kanban stores
  the bounded terminal scalars on the task run; only verified success may mark
  the task complete, publish completion proof, learn completion memory, award
  XP, or advance a collaborative dependent agent. Partial children stay blocked
  and open, while failed/cancelled children close with their exact terminal
  status and stop the parent sequence.
- For genuine two- or three-action requests, the Chat planner no
  longer lets a first-match conversational handler silently consume only one
  ask. It routes the intact request once through OpenSwan with a bounded A1-A3
  ledger, grouped `and`/`then` dependencies, and an explicit all-actions
  completion rule. More than three accepted asks stop for one clarification
  without dispatch. Risk and approval use the strictest child/whole-request
  floor, while terminal transport forces the
  contract through the batch OpenSwan runtime instead of ordinary talk
  streaming or a specialized single-intent mode. Existing computer-task
  compilers and the atomic Office-agent create-and-attach executor remain on
  their specialized paths; no per-child replay loop was introduced.
- `run.report_action_outcomes` is force-advertised only for that contract turn,
  is prompted as the one final report, and executes as a singleton barrier. It
  structurally acknowledges one bounded outcome per A-id plus exact earlier
  provider tool-use ids, performs
  no external or mutation I/O, and explicitly returns no completion decision.
  `openSwanMultiActionCompletionCore.ts` independently compares the report to
  runtime-owned ordered tool events. Evidence must use an operation-specific
  completion tool; mutations must carry authoritative mutation and exact target
  binding. A derived assistant-authored text action may instead use the deferred
  `run.publish_action_artifact` path with one exact current A-id, one allowlisted
  artifact kind, and bounded redacted title/content. The runtime admits that
  publisher call as evidence only when it is the single pre-report claim for
  that action and the canonical `agent_run_artifacts` insert returns the exact
  successful row; the final reporter must cite that earlier provider tool-use
  id. A pure draft may use that artifact alone, but a source-grounded summary,
  analysis, comparison, or recommendation requires both exact supporting read
  evidence and the durable artifact. An explicit file, SaaS, app, browser,
  desktop, or other external destination stays mutation-bound; publication
  grants no mutation authority and cannot replace operation-specific mutation
  proof. Artifact content stays in the canonical row while tool evidence and
  Chat/Room A# snapshots remain value-free. Chat stores an opaque
  `canonicalArtifactId`, the canonical content digest, and a truncated inline
  display copy. It batch rehydrates only an exact
  artifact-id/run/circle/title/A-id/kind row whose independently recomputed
  SHA-256 agrees with both the row and pointer; completion never trusts the
  message copy. Unmapped reads, unsupported mutations, and derived
  work without the required read and/or durable typed receipt stay unavailable.
  Missing, duplicated,
  unknown, future, cross-owned, status-mismatched, dependency-inverted, invalid,
  unavailable, pending, or blocked evidence yields partial
  `action_coverage_incomplete`; an explicitly failed action yields
  `action_coverage_failed`; the reporter's own event is never evidence. Contract
  tool dispatch is sequential where dependencies require it. The bounded action
  snapshot is written through a per-run serialized optimistic CAS with exact-row
  readback; a missing run or unverified write becomes `persistence_unverified`.
  The same terminal reasons and value-free A# projection survive Chat/Room reload.
- Feed's Active Runs widget now requests and defensively rechecks the canonical
  active-only subset: only fresh `planning` or `running` rows are Active.
  Queued, paused, approval-waiting, timestamp-less, and stale rows stay
  non-active, while accepted connected-agent handoffs remain visible in their
  own `ACCEPTED HANDOFFS` / `COMPLETION UNVERIFIED` lane.
- Browser-backed plan creation now reports `deferred`/waiting approval. A plan
  card that is ready for approval is not the requested browser task's terminal
  completion.
- Generic approval cards now continue the exact original Chat task rather than
  posting an internal "approval granted" instruction back through the ordinary
  router. Chat groups only same-run/same-requester rows, rebuilds one exact
  value-free continuation binding, preserves composer attachments until the
  user sends them separately, and forces the original task and source ledger
  through OpenSwan. Typed, legacy, and local-batch handlers authorize only the
  listed approval ids and digests after exact source-run/user/circle/thread
  revalidation and atomic consume. A selected connected agent, Plan mode,
  technical prompt classification, newest-row fallback, or a similarly titled
  approval cannot intercept or widen that continuation.
- `scripts/openswan-terminal-outcome-contract-smoketest.ts` exercises the
  terminal/precedence matrix with hostile opposite-sounding prose. The
  multi-action core/report/artifact-evidence/read-evidence/wiring/
  semantic-evidence/routing/CAS, ordinary-required-tool truth, exact approval
  resume binding/Chat routing, exact-resume locator, Feed active/terminal,
  Room persistence, Mission,
  Chat, planner, multi-intent, and transport smokes cover their
  projections. Focused smokes and app typecheck are current; no live provider
  turn, authenticated cross-surface refresh, cross-process resume, deployment,
  or production database transition is claimed.

#### 2026-08-13 Office catalog personalization, floor lifecycle, and bounded kit planning

- The visible addon catalog now consumes a versioned, bounded, user-and-circle-scoped
  local preference record for Favorites and the newest 20 recent types. It also
  exposes Recent and Needs Attention scopes without creating another catalog or
  §37 server persistence path; malformed and unsupported preference payloads
  fail closed.
- Floor creation caps at 10, opens the new floor in edit mode, and supports a
  sanitized inline rename. Floor chips expose selected state and item/agent
  counts; destructive deletion/clear paths stay confirmed, save failure is a
  labelled retry action, operation status uses a polite live region, and nested
  widget controls leave pointer and accessibility traversal while editing.
- `planOfficeRoomKit` owns a detached, deterministic, bounded collision scan.
  It checks canonical catalog sizes, rotation-aware rectangles, internal and
  existing-item overlap, bounds, capacity, and fresh ids, then returns typed
  malformed/capacity/no-space/scan-limit failures. `OfficeTab` passes the
  canonical 16px grid and 900×970 bounds from `officeFloorLayout.ts`; the pure
  helper does not create a second grid owner.
- Office layout cache writes now use one user-and-circle envelope, a serialized
  queue, and exact readback before a snapshot is eligible for server dispatch.
  The editor remains behind the authenticated scoped-hydration boundary: an
  unresolved `null` scope is never treated as hydrated, so authentication
  arrival cannot reset an editor session that was exposed too early.
  The server writer retains only the newest pending snapshot for that exact
  scope: a busy call latches a re-drain, an older non-conflict failure advances
  once to a newer edit, and an older conflict pauses for a fresh read without
  deleting the newer edit. Layout reads and writes abort after 12 seconds, so a
  hung transport cannot indefinitely strand later verified local changes.
  The local-cache queue owns its own deadline too, so a storage adapter that
  ignores the caller's race cannot poison every later backup. Queue freshness
  now includes the active write, rejected transports restore their exact item,
  and SAVED requires both the newest version and unchanged mutation epoch.
  Retry rebinds a preserved pending snapshot to the freshly checked same-user
  token; an edit during retry verification invalidates the older snapshot.
  Optional appearance/preferences timeouts degrade independently and cannot
  discard a successful authoritative layout read.
  Server responses pass a strict typed receipt interpreter, and same-version
  divergent JSON is rejected by the source §37 function plus the pending
  `20260813140000_office_layout_exact_save_receipt.sql` follow-up for databases
  that already installed §37. That follow-up also revokes authenticated raw
  layout DML, rejects unsafe/far-future versions, and binds acknowledgement runs
  to their circle with server-owned acknowledgement/expiry timestamps. Until
  that trigger is deployed, compatibility upserts carry a bounded 30-day window
  so re-dismissing an expired historical row renews it instead of remaining inert.
- Async confirmation, image-processing, floor-preset, and save-retry
  continuations bind to the initiating user/circle scope and layout generation.
  A later navigation, floor change, or edit cancels the stale continuation
  instead of mutating the newly active workspace.
- Preset reads and mutations are circle-bound at both UI and persistence
  boundaries. A slower prior-circle response is discarded; apply/delete reject
  mismatched records, and delete predicates plus verifies both id and circle.
  Preset-applied rosters become manual user state. Automatic floors
  idempotently distribute only live agents not claimed by a manual floor, so a
  bridge refresh cannot overwrite a captured assignment.
- Status/activity-only roster reorder preserves existing automatic occupancy.
  Departed agents vacate and newly live agents fill only open capacity, while
  manual claims continue to win; runtime display order may change without
  generating a user-layout version or server write.
- Telegram credentials, agent names, appearances, notes, budget, idle behavior,
  and filter preferences now hydrate through exact authenticated scope. Private
  local copies use user-and-circle keys, ownerless legacy keys are never
  imported, and profile writes enter immutable per-user lanes that validate
  bounded JSON, reject retired accounts before dispatch, and preserve unknown
  same-user timeout ordering without blocking another signed-in account.
- Hydration and every editor geometry mutation now share one center-rotation-aware
  constraint. Pointer cancellation restores the preview without committing,
  document listeners are removed on cancel/unmount, and Whack-a-Mole joins Farm
  and Pet in the nested-control wrapper exclusion.
- `scripts/office-authenticated-local-e2e.mjs` adds an explicit opt-in canary
  using a disposable authenticated user/circle and ephemeral headless system
  Chrome rather than the persistent browser bridge. It preflights cleanup
  authority and always attempts verified cleanup. Its 2026-08-13 receipt proved
  exact scoped-local plus authenticated-server version-and-payload convergence,
  real trusted-mouse Desk move and resize with exact snapping and undo/redo,
  rotated/duplicated Desk geometry, reload reconstruction,
  favorites/recents, edit history, floor add/rename/delete, the exact six-type
  Focus Lab placement, compact semantic placed-item nudge/resize, a 533px
  visible compact floor with no document overflow, reduced motion, zero
  page/server errors, and zero-record cleanup. Deployed, native,
  screen-reader, provider/OAuth, negative-RLS, and cross-device
  §37 proof remain pending.
  The canary now also verifies the exact route and Supabase project, records a
  SHA-256 manifest of the exercised Expo bundle resources, writes artifacts
  only beneath a validated real directory, and captures bounded response plus
  screenshot evidence on failure. A fresh production export passed that
  desktop/mobile canary with zero uncaught/essential-server errors and verified
  cleanup on 2026-08-13.

#### 2026-08-13 Office interaction and addon-action hardening

- Compact editing now uses independently collapsible Catalog, Kits, and Items
  trays plus 44px semantic placed-item selectors, so tiny and reloaded objects
  can be selected without pixel hunting while the floor keeps meaningful space.
- The terminal dock and unrelated bridge strips leave the layout during edit
  mode. This fixes a verified obstruction where the dock covered a Desk resize
  handle; the authenticated canary now hit-tests the handle before performing
  trusted-mouse move/resize, exact snapped commit, undo, and redo.
- Interactive addons sit above decorative agent sprites. Link-only widgets
  open their own setup flow when unconfigured, reject invalid/non-HTTPS input
  without clearing saved setup, and use setup rather than connection copy.
- Button Panel and Launch Pad now stage a visible command and require one exact
  agent target before dispatch. Their first click never broadcasts to `@all`.
- Bridge controls and compact agent-panel navigation now expose explicit
  expanded, disabled, busy, alert/live-region, and 44px target semantics.

#### 2026-08-13 Office command and integration release hardening

- Button Panel and Launch Pad targets now resolve in the durable terminal
  identity domain. Canonical virtual BlackSwan is explicit; every other target
  is an exact enabled/connected owner UUID row, ambiguous publish identities
  are omitted, and OpenSwan rows additionally require a current private
  session binding. Presentation IDs can no longer look selectable and then be
  silently stripped during persistence.
- The picker and full terminal keep one canonical selected-ID array. One final
  resolver atomically derives both compatible single- and multi-target columns;
  a stale/presentation-only identity fails before persistence instead of
  silently becoming `@all`. A mounted dispatcher is also required before any
  command insert, so a terminal with no execution owner keeps the draft and
  produces no orphaned durable command.
- Furniture review and the full Office terminal await durable command
  persistence, retain the draft when it fails, and invoke through the same
  claimant path only after receiving a valid message id. Realtime broadcast is
  advisory: wake-up failure returns the saved id and warning instead of
  replaying or losing the command. Launch effects and Button preset advancement
  happen only after persistence succeeds.
- Direct and Realtime dispatch now read the same latest exact-target ref. The
  Realtime listener stays mounted over a stable owner-scoped superset instead
  of resubscribing during session polling, then revalidates the exact live
  connection and private binding at delivery time. Duplicate connection IDs,
  not only duplicate publish names, are omitted before selection.
- Edit mode hides the terminal presentation without unmounting its local state
  or realtime subscriptions. Static decorations sit behind agents in read
  mode; actionable tools and all edit-mode items remain above them. The terminal
  command input and Send action expose stable explicit accessible names, and the
  authenticated canary proves an unsent draft survives the mode round trip.
- Calendar and Email setup now scopes asynchronous results to exact generation,
  circle, floor, item, service, and provider identities. Provider mutations are
  serialized, unavailable status does not fabricate disconnection, stale or
  out-of-order results cannot patch a newer target, and provider-wide revoke
  invalidates every loaded widget sharing that credential.
- Direct Calendar/Email reads have an independent item/provider generation;
  close, floor/circle change, provider switch, and newer reads invalidate stale
  callbacks. A provider switch clears the old provider's cached event/mail and
  live-state payload through the canonical reset before the new account can be
  saved as connected.
- The OAuth Edge source allowlists only `calendar` and `email`, safely unions a
  new service request with stored same-provider granted scopes, persists that
  normalized union after refresh, and refreshes before `/status`, calendar, or
  email reads. An expired or otherwise unrefreshable stored credential returns
  `reconnectRequired`, which the client renders as `reconnect_required`, rather
  than claiming the mere credential row is connected. Provider bodies and raw
  token errors do not cross the response boundary.
- `20260813190000_atomic_oauth_credential_store.sql` replaces the generic
  client-readable Google/Microsoft OAuth row with a service-only encrypted
  control record. Authorization callbacks are fenced by a monotonic intent
  epoch and credential revision; refreshes claim one bounded lease before the
  provider call and commit through epoch/revision/claim CAS; disconnect retains
  a secret-free tombstone that invalidates every older callback or refresh.
  Both access and refresh tokens are encrypted, generic BYOK RPC/RLS paths
  reserve the OAuth namespace, rotating refresh tokens are preserved only for
  the same stable provider subject, and source plus disposable PostgreSQL tests
  cover concurrent consent, lease contention, account switching, legacy
  migration, and disconnect-vs-refresh resurrection. Google uses incremental
  authorization; Microsoft's documented omitted `scope` token response may
  inherit only the exact scopes held in the one-time server state. Yahoo/AOL is
  no longer presented as a real inbox because no provider adapter exists.
- Personal Figma connection now uses the same user-gesture-safe popup and exact
  callback relay boundary while remaining a distinct provider flow. The Edge
  binds the exact complete browser state to one-time SQL state, uses PKCE S256 plus
  HTTP Basic client authentication for exchange and refresh, stores tokens only
  in the encrypted service control plane, and sends Figma API requests with a
  server-side Bearer token. The browser receives only a bounded file reference
  summary. `file_content:read` is the sole accepted grant; circle integration
  credentials and saved Board links remain explicitly separate from the
  personal account. Intent/revision fences, refresh lease/CAS, stable-account
  checks, disconnect tombstones, bounded provider bodies, and trusted-origin
  303 callback redirects fail closed on stale or ambiguous work. A pending
  authorization suppresses background refresh rotation so its callback
  revision cannot be invalidated underneath the popup. Claiming retains the
  exact callback-state row under a one-minute lease; the credential path
  self-heals only the exact missing or expired state, so an abandoned callback
  cannot leave refresh authority stuck and an active callback is not erased.
  Client authorize, status, and disconnect operations have a total 15-second
  deadline; same-generation status checks deduplicate, stale panel lifecycles
  cannot mutate current UI, and an unknown disconnect never reports success.
  Documented file-endpoint credential rejection and definitive refresh
  rejection remove
  only the exact rejected credential; a newer connection wins, while a pending
  reconnect callback keeps its exact fence and can still commit. Transient
  provider failures remain retryable, invalid-client configuration failures do
  not erase user credentials, multi-link reads share a five-second pre-send
  budget, and oversized roots request a specific frame/node instead of retrying
  forever. Provider/attachment fields enter model context
  only through the canonical readable untrusted-content fence, and fixed
  recovery guidance is derived separately from a trusted local enum.
- Focused target, advisory wake-up, UI wiring, out-of-order OAuth, refresh, and
  scope-union tests are current, including the 126-assertion shared popup
  boundary, 32-assertion Google/Microsoft SQL contract, 117-assertion Figma
  boundary, and 68-assertion Figma SQL/parity contract. The authenticated
  Office canary still covers
  pointer, responsive, persistence, and pre-Send behavior. Section 42 and the
  matching Google/Microsoft Edge source plus section 43 and the Figma Edge
  source are hardened but not claimed deployed. Migrations `20260813190000`
  and `20260813200000` are not applied, and the live `figma-oauth` endpoint
  currently returns 404; required Edge secrets,
  real-provider OAuth/file reads, and authenticated external-agent dispatch
  remain explicit proof gaps.

#### 2026-08-17 Circle-global idle automation claim and Chat nudge opt-in

- All five shared-Chat behaviors — Streak Guardian, Circle Pulse, Morning
  Briefing, Weekly Retro, and Goal Pace Tracker — require a separate explicit
  `sharedChatOptIn` and the configured Office owner account. The old Tier-1
  auto-on states normalize off, shared writers run no more often than daily
  (Weekly Retro remains weekly), and browser time is not scheduling authority.
- Office does not start idle work from transient defaults. The dedicated idle
  lifecycle waits for the exact user/circle preference read (including a valid
  no-row result) and membership proof, and it no longer restarts when a bridge
  connection or display name changes. Exact local state is a scoped cache and
  display projection, never cross-client execution authority.
- `claim_idle_behavior_run_v1` reserves `(circle_id,behavior_id)` atomically
  before any status, activity, message, memory mutation, bridge command, or
  Edge execution. The
  SECURITY DEFINER RPC verifies `auth.uid()` membership, uses the server clock,
  normalizes shared-Chat cooldowns to at least 1,440 minutes, and returns a
  value-bounded receipt. Direct authenticated table DML is revoked and RLS is
  forced. A rejected, unavailable, or malformed claim fails closed with zero
  behavior effects, including across tabs, devices, members, reloads, or a
  replacement after a committed message.
- Migration `20260817120000_circle_idle_behavior_claims.sql` is mirrored
  byte-for-byte as §46. Source, exact-parity, ordering, hydration, and focused
  scheduler checks are current 2026-08-17. Section 46 is **not applied**; live
  multi-member contention, deployed scheduling, and server catalog proof remain
  pending.

#### 2026-08-14 Office exact-authority continuation and build guard

- Office computer-task state, agent-plan queues, and Profile computer-use
  history now load through captured user/circle/bearer/generation APIs. A scope
  change clears the prior view synchronously, late results fail their current-
  authority fence, and Realtime only schedules an exact durable reload.
- Customize provider-key and custom-theme list/create/test/delete hooks use the
  same exact scope and clear on retirement. Workspace profile/adaptation reads,
  writes, and activity counters are partitioned by exact user and circle rather
  than an ownerless local fallback. Hidden-agent suppression is also keyed by
  user, circle, and normalized agent name.
- OAuth/Figma disconnect work is abortable and generation-fenced. Terminal
  deletion verifies one exact returned sender/circle row before local removal;
  direct and listener invocation carry immutable exact execution authority plus
  an abort signal, and post-dispatch uncertainty remains non-replayable
  `outcome_unknown`.
- Phone/Messenger metadata and provider secrets are keyed by exact user and
  circle, its bearer subject is verified before every public operation, and
  account-generation retirement aborts provider requests and synchronously
  unmounts contacts, messages, and drafts. Ownerless legacy credentials are
  never imported into an exact Office session.
- The idle scheduler verifies its captured bearer, supplies it explicitly on
  every Supabase query/mutation and Edge invocation, scopes local configuration
  by user and circle, and guards shared-memory writes. Office continuously
  revalidates membership through Realtime plus a bounded periodic lease; loss
  retires the generation and clears private state before any retry.
- `chatPlanApprovalAuthorityCore.ts` is the dependency-free shared object-
  capability owner used by app and Edge-imported approval paths. Only a branded
  in-process object matching the complete expected scope can authorize one plan;
  JSON or copied metadata remains inert.
- Production dependency overrides remain deliberately narrow: `nanoid@3.3.18`
  and Jayson/xcode `uuid@11.1.1`, while rpc-websockets keeps its declared UUID
  major. The pre-export Expo asset guard reads only bounded magic signatures,
  refuses symlinks, and blocks repository-controlled ICNS/JXL/HEIF/HEIC assets
  before Metro reaches the vulnerable parser.
- Source review, focused smokes, `check:office-addons`, both typecheck suites,
  a clean production export, bundle scan, and authenticated frozen-export
  desktop/mobile canary passed on 2026-08-14. They remain distinct from
  deployed-site, real-provider, native-secret-store, and multi-account proof.
  The upstream `image-size` advisories have no patched release and remain
  build-time audit debt. The latest read-only Office audit also still marks
  Vault server enforcement/recent-auth, agent-connect tokens, MCP
  administration, and schedules/run-history mutations P0; do not call the
  entire Office exact-authority complete.

#### 2026-08-13 Office exact runtime continuations and membership gate

- Office does not hydrate private local or server state until the captured
  bearer verifies its subject and an exact `(user_id,circle_id)` membership
  row. A scoped cache is only an optimization; an empty or failed membership
  read keeps the private subtree closed and exposes a bounded retry.
- Office connection load/save captures user, circle, bearer, and authority
  generation. Exact envelopes contain sanitized metadata, connection tokens
  remain in verified protected local-secret storage, removed connections delete
  their exact secrets, and retired generations fail closed. Office no longer
  treats the app-wide auto-connect cache or singleton session list as authority;
  the older connection APIs remain compatibility paths for non-Office callers.
- Terminal inserts verify the bearer subject, use that bearer on the exact
  circle/sender write, validate the returned durable row, and issue an immutable
  message-plus-target receipt. Both the direct path and the account-bound
  listener require that receipt and a current generation before dispatch;
  realtime broadcast is an advisory wake-up and cannot authorize replay.
- HITL and run-approval mutations capture exact user, circle, token, and
  generation, preserve pending-only idempotence, and validate returned
  resolution rows before notifying Office. Exact Office mode does not route an
  approval through the generic side-effect worker or persist a standing
  auto-approve rule.
- Calendar/Email and Figma status, connect, read, and disconnect continuations
  use the captured Office bearer and generation guard. A popup closes and its
  result is ignored after account retirement. The shared OAuth helpers retain
  session-refresh compatibility only for explicitly non-Office callers.
- The idle scheduler is owned by the mounting Office lifecycle: it records
  user, circle, and generation, checks the captured token-bearing Office guard
  around asynchronous work, returns one exact cleanup that cancels both timers,
  and drains any retired predecessor before replacement work begins. The
  circle-only stop overload remains a compatibility API, not Office authority.
- These are source and focused-smoke claims dated 2026-08-13. They do not prove
  live two-account/two-tab races, native protected-secret cleanup, real-provider
  OAuth, or deployed behavior.

#### 2026-08-13 Office exact private-state and account-switch boundary

- `office_user_preferences` is the canonical owner-private, circle-scoped
  server row for names, appearances, notes, budget/idle/filter settings, and
  non-secret Telegram metadata. Authenticated clients receive RLS-scoped reads
  and one allowlisted atomic patch RPC; direct table mutation is revoked.
- The validator rejects unknown top-level fields, recursive secret-bearing
  keys, malformed Telegram metadata, oversized documents, and malformed nested
  appearance/note/config values. Patch receipts are value-free and return only
  acceptance, revision, and server timestamp.
- The migration removes private keys from peer-readable
  `profiles.office_preferences`, empties deprecated
  `profiles.agent_appearance`, and installs narrow triggers that prevent older
  clients from reintroducing those private values. Telegram bot tokens remain
  only in exact user/circle verified local secret storage.
- Office captures immutable user, circle, access-token, and authority-generation
  identity for preference writes, roster reads, agent identity, presence,
  heartbeat, and session/tag cache work. Stale completions and cleanup from a
  retired account cannot update or tear down the current account. A full
  private-subtree hydration gate prevents prior-account UI from flashing while
  the new exact scope loads.
- `20260813220000_office_user_preferences.sql` is mirrored byte-for-byte as
  §45. Static, exact-parity, exact-scope runtime, queue/deadline, and disposable
  PostgreSQL behavior smokes pass locally. The migration is **not applied**;
  live negative-RLS, two-account/two-tab races, native secret-store logout,
  cross-device convergence, and deployed UI behavior remain unverified.

#### 2026-08-12 Office addon truth and reversible editor

- `officeConfig.ts` now owns one exhaustive registry for all 81 placeable Office
  items. The `FurnitureType` union derives from that list, and focused coverage
  requires exactly one catalog definition plus complete interaction and product
  metadata for every type.
- Connected-looking widgets carry explicit `local`, `demo`, `setup`, `live`,
  `stale`, or `error` state. Saving a link or channel records local setup; it no
  longer invents connected, playing, live-stream, participant, repository,
  weather, market, design-file, calendar, or email evidence. Actual provider
  reads timestamp live data, and live state without fresh evidence ages to
  `Update needed`.
- The editor searches the full catalog, filters categories plus
  Ready/Setup/Demo/Local state, and can place five deterministic bounded room
  kits: Agent Ops, Focus Lab, Launch Room, Review Room, and Social Lounge.
  These helpers operate on detached floor snapshots and continue through the
  existing Office layout persistence owner.
- A bounded local history (30 edits by default, never more than 60) makes item
  placement, duplicate, move, resize, rotation, layer order, reset size, kit
  placement, and confirmed removal undoable and redoable. A bounded per-floor
  item-state cache keeps configuration intact across undo-add/redo, while Farm
  upgrades and progression persist through the allowlisted layout contract.
  Mobile edit mode now
  exposes the floor. Web keyboard and accessibility actions provide selection,
  activation, grid nudge, and deletion alternatives to pointer dragging.
- `StatusPicker` now reports save/loading/error state and `WorldClockBar` uses
  IANA timezone formatting, DST-safe refresh, invalid-zone fallback, and
  accessible labels. Both components remain intentionally unmounted from the
  main dashboard; this change does not reverse the personal status/timer strip
  omission or claim a visible team clock bar. The placeable `world_clock` item
  is the visible computed-time surface.
- `npm run check:office-addons` covers the registry, catalog querying, status
  aging, bounded history, room-kit placement, exhaustive renderer/UI wiring,
  allowlisted layout validation, dashboard serialization, and app typecheck.
  This is source/focused-smoke evidence only;
  live responsive browser/native, screen-reader, pointer/drag, provider/OAuth,
  and cross-device §37 behavior remain unverified.

#### 2026-08-11 Office dashboard truth and per-circle floor persistence

- Circle Runs and per-agent run panels share the same freshness clock. Only
  fresh `planning` and `running` rows are Active. Queued, paused, and
  approval-waiting rows stay under Other; processing rows older than the
  30-minute liveness window become `Stale · Not Active`. A heartbeat-opted dead process
  may be reaped, but a legacy stale row is never called complete and can only
  be explicitly closed as Cancelled.
- Office attention acknowledgements are durable and revision-scoped, and stale
  paused/waiting episodes no longer recreate an urgent banner forever.
- Layout state is private per user/circle, saved through serialized monotonic
  writes, and backed by a circle-scoped local cache. Complete floor presets
  include theme, agents, furniture, tools/integrations, labels, and interactive
  state. Source/focused tests and disposable PostgreSQL behavior are current;
  §37 is catalog-ready per the user's 2026-08-12 SQL Editor result; live
  authenticated cross-device/UI verification remains pending.

#### 2026-08-11 Office cost stability across login

- Office no longer promotes local identity lifetime cost or cached cumulative
  session cost into a field labelled today. The session meter remains available
  solely for delta sync; one exact durable Office row owns daily and lifetime
  presentation, with provider fallback allowed only when both sides are unique.
- Agent cards now render daily cost when labelled today. The token card and
  whiteboard tracked-spend ledger use server-backed rolling 24h, 7d, and 30d
  windows; a failed login-time usage read retains the prior snapshot and retries
  instead of flashing or caching a false zero.
- `smoke:office-cost-stability` covers exact and ambiguous attribution, daily
  reset behavior, identity/cache hydration, session snapshot persistence, and
  Office source wiring. This is source/focused-smoke evidence; no live provider
  billing reconciliation or cross-device visual pass is claimed.
- The main Office dashboard no longer renders the running-cost/reset trip meter
  or the personal presence/status-note/timer picker. Durable spend still feeds
  the token tracker and whiteboard instead of occupying the workspace header.

#### 2026-08-06 application security incident review and hardening

- The durable, date-bounded evidence ledger is
  [`SECURITY_REVIEW_2026-08-06.md`](./SECURITY_REVIEW_2026-08-06.md). It is
  authoritative for what was verified live versus source-only in this pass;
  it does not replace the ownership or SQL status tables in this roadmap.
- Live remediation closed anonymous collaboration/circle reads, moved public
  circle discovery/join to safe RPC projections, made 13 views security
  invokers, and reduced the `public`-schema `SECURITY DEFINER` catalog to zero
  `PUBLIC`/anonymous execute grants. All 101 remain service-role callable;
  exactly 52 audited signatures are authenticated-callable with no extras.
- An anonymously executable credential-key helper was treated as a key
  compromise. Its execute grants were closed and both stored credential rows
  were atomically re-encrypted under a rotated key without printing key or
  plaintext material. One legacy circle invite/API credential was also rotated
  without reading its values.
- Live Auth now uses the production site URL, explicit production/development
  redirect allowlist, 8-character lowercase/uppercase/digit policy, HIBP
  screening, and recent reauthentication for password changes. Email
  auto-confirm remains enabled; production SMTP and CAPTCHA remain absent.
- The source login bootstrap, recovery/reset, generic error, centralized
  logout cleanup, native SecureStore, app-origin OAuth relay, public-env build
  gate, Edge auth boundaries, and stricter Netlify headers require the exact
  current frontend artifact to be deployed and reverified before they are live
  claims. Real mailbox/IdP flows, private task-image delivery, claimant-bound
  Office execution, device-bound bridge pairing, two-client RLS races, and
  migration-ledger reconciliation remain open.
- One initial anonymous `aggregate-analytics` probe caused one recomputation/
  upsert before that endpoint was fixed. No other review-induced production
  write is known.

- Top-level `ErrorBoundary` + per-tab `ErrorBoundary` (CircleDetailScreen LazyTab).
- `safeGetUser` / `safeGetSession` migrated across Wallet, Challenges, Members, Digest, Profile, EditProfile, CheckIn, CreateCircle, CircleSettings, and CustomizePanel (×5 sites). Remaining unguarded sites are all inside `OfficeTab.tsx` / `RoomsTab.tsx` which will be migrated during the P1 splits in `OPTIMIZATION_PLAN.md`.
- Office localhost gating + `BridgeUnavailableBanner` (no more silent blank Office on prod).
- Global unhandled-rejection logger (`errorReporter.ts`).
- Confirm dialogs on delete circle / leave circle / disconnect wallet / delete theme / delete mission task.

#### 2026-05-09 app review / agent-doc cleanup

- Agent entrypoint normalized: `AGENTS.md` is the start file; `AGENT.md`,
  `CLAUDE.md`, `Gemini.md`, `MEMORY.md`, and `UC_APP_STACK_REFERENCE.md` now
  point back to this roadmap instead of competing with it.
- Current TypeScript baseline: `npm run typecheck` passes.
- Provider marketplace is now part of the runtime architecture. Current
  ownership includes `llmProviders`, `circleIntegrations`,
  `serviceProfileSouls`, `crossProviderRouter`, `universalInvoke`,
  `billingPriority`, `llm-proxy`, `swanbot-ai`, and DB provider constraints.

  Groq, Google AI, Mistral AI, Cohere, Perplexity, Together AI, Fireworks AI,

- Persisted chat metadata is a bounded envelope for source, routing, usage,

  explicit: transcript rows feed persistence/model/memory/archive; ephemeral

  `getSession()` directly. New code should use `authSession` helpers or caught
