#### 2026-05-29 app automation research and buildout quality gate

- `appAutomationControlSurfaces` stores primary-source metadata for official
  Adobe, Autodesk/APS, SOLIDWORKS, Rhino, Playwright, Chrome, Apple, and
  Microsoft automation references: source type, review date, primary use, and
  must-confirm checks. Connected app-capability buildout prompts include the
  same `Official App Automation Research` block before implementation
  instructions.
- `appAutomationControlSurfaces` also produces a typed route decision for the
  next app action: ready to execute, needs observation, needs approval, needs
  user action, or needs connected-agent buildout. The decision records the
  chosen control surface, skipped stronger surfaces, missing confirmations,
  missing approvals, source refs, verification, fail-safe rules, and bounded
  next steps before chat mutates another app. `chatComputerRequestRouter`
  carries that decision in the hidden chat route block, while
  `computerAppPreflight` converts it into warning/blocker/fix-action state
  before dispatch. `chatComputerHandoffContext` and `persistedChatMetadata`
  preserve a compact route-decision summary for refresh and recovery.
- `genericAppNavigator` is the canonical unfamiliar-app task-family classifier.
  Use `buildGenericAppNavigatorRouteContext` instead of rebuilding target-app or
  task-family detection inside older strategy/buildout files. Its user-readable
  labels flow through `appAutomationControlSurfaces`,
  `chatComputerRequestRouter`, `chatComputerRequestUx`,
  `chatComputerHandoffContext`, and `computerTaskEvidenceContract`, so recovery
  and connected-agent buildout receive context such as "Ableton Live
  file/save/export work" instead of a generic native-app bucket while successful
  chat remains quiet.
- As of 2026-08-05, use `buildGenericAppSemanticWorkflow` for the intact
  unfamiliar-app request rather than splitting its clauses into separate chat
  asks. Its maximum-ten-checkpoint program is semantic-only and ends in proof;
  it contains no coordinate target or implied raw bridge authority. One bounded
  review may cover reversible non-secret field/menu/toggle checkpoints, while
  exact runtime receipts remain mandatory and every persistent, external,
  destructive, credential, permission, payment, or ambiguous checkpoint keeps
  its exact floor. An uncertain post-dispatch result is non-complete and
  non-replayable.
- Engineering/CAD requests now get a dedicated control-surface ladder before
  generic native-app fallback: AutoCAD API/AutoLISP, Fusion API scripts/add-ins,
  SOLIDWORKS API, RhinoCommon, Revit API, Inventor API/iLogic-style routes, APS
  cloud automation when upload/credential/output approval is available, then
  accessibility/menu/coordinate fallback and connected-agent adapter buildout.
- Engineering/CAD runbooks now break those requests into hidden
  observe/approve/act/verify/recover/stop recipes for inspect/measure, 2D
  drafting, dimension/layer/title-block updates, model/BIM edits, export/plot,
  and batch conversion. The runbooks are injected into SwanBot/OpenSwan and
  computer-task dispatch, then compacted into handoff/persisted metadata without
  exposing local paths in chat.
- As of 2026-06-23, connected-agent app-capability buildout prompts also embed
  the Engineering/CAD operation runbooks for AutoCAD, MATLAB/Simulink,
  SOLIDWORKS, and similar tasks. Missing CAD/MATLAB/SolidWorks adapters now ask
  Codex-style repair agents for the same observe/approve/act/verify/recover
  contract, source refs, smoke proof, and bounded retry plan that the runtime
  will enforce after the capability is ready.
- App-capability buildout results are not `ready_to_retry` unless they include
  a chosen control surface, credible official/repo source refs, passing
  verification, and a retry plan. Incomplete results persist as blocked
  evidence gaps, show the missing proof, and do not trigger the automatic retry.

#### 2026-06-01 worktree quality and ownership guard

- `agentDevelopmentStandards` now builds a worktree-quality checklist for
  delegated agents. It accepts changed paths or `git status --porcelain=v1
  -uall` lines, maps files back to canonical owners, flags dirty-tree,
  untracked-canonical-file, missing-owner, parallel-path, verification-gap, and
  cross-surface risks, and recommends focused smokes plus typecheck/diff checks.
  The hidden standards handoff block includes that checklist, and the normal
  standards prompt helpers accept `changedPaths` / `hasUnrelatedChanges`, so
  Codex, Claude Code, Cursor Composer, Gemini, and custom connected agents can
  extend existing owners before creating more parallel paths.
- The worktree owner map now accounts for the current agent/chat/app automation
  tree with named reasons for package scripts/config, chat planning/metadata,
  OpenSwan runtime, app automation control surfaces, chat computer runtime,
  product UI surfaces, second-brain/research surfaces, planning docs, canonical
  docs, agent/runtime SQL, and generic app navigation. A representative smoke
  prevents those clusters from falling back to generic/unmapped owners again.
- `computerCapabilityExpansion` adds a pure, smoke-tested planning layer above
  the live capability audit. It converts user computer/app/browser requests into
  build lanes for browser semantic actionability, browser protocol inspection,
  desktop semantic control, app-native adapters, local-file grants, and
  connected-agent buildout, with official source refs, proof requirements, and
  focused smoke commands. This keeps future "make it able to do more tasks"
  work tied to concrete build actions instead of broad prose.

#### 2026-07-26 sealed computer-app canaries and durable exact-call ledger

- `agentExecutionCore` treats each provider tool-use id as a run-wide call
  capability. Empty, oversized, control-character, same-round duplicate, or
  resumed/later-round reused ids reject the complete requested round before
  transcript append or handler entry. Every guarded call receives the exact
  `toolName`, `toolUseId`, and iteration; ids are never fabricated.
- OpenSwan approvals carry genuine durable receipts for run-scoped, consumed
  cross-run, and category-auto decisions. Category auto records an exact
  `auto_approved` row first; missing row ids and lookup errors block. Hidden
  telemetry carries only an issued receipt and privacy-safe digest, never the
  canonical approval key or mutation arguments.
- The sealed typed runtime now has three browser consumers:
  `browser.fill_field`, `browser.set_toggle`, and `browser.select_option`.
  Each observes and resolves one exact ElementHandle before approval, issues a
  short-lived single-use capability, binds process/context/live-document and
  keyed semantic/structural identity, executes at most one non-submit mutation,
  and accepts completion only from redacted same-target after-state proof.
  Fill excludes credentials/secrets and verifies exact server-side value
  equality; toggle supports only a positively identified local checkbox,
  switch, or radio; select supports only a native single-value select and one
  exact option value/label. Navigation, close, restart, expiry, replay,
  detachment, or target drift requires fresh observation.
- Raw/direct bypasses are closed for those three stateful controls, not for all
  browser automation. Generic click inspects the resolved handle and refuses
  state controls, selects/comboboxes/listboxes/options, their labels, and
  descendants; stateful fill/toggle/select execution enters only through the
  sealed runtime. The vault/origin-gated credential tool stays a separate lane.
- Native `desktop.click_element` adds one deliberately narrow semantic-press
  canary. It requires a fresh indexed accessibility observation, exact
  app/PID/path/role/label/fingerprint agreement, genuine approval, one
  target-bound press, and a refreshed exact-target semantic diff. It does not
  authorize general desktop coordinate actions or arbitrary app mutations.
- `agent_action_calls` closes the source-level cross-process replay gap for
  these five canaries. The client and SECURITY DEFINER RPCs bind user, circle,
  persisted run, tool, provider call id, action id, argument/contract
  fingerprints, and idempotency key while excluding raw args, selectors, URLs,
  and content. The state machine is `claimed → dispatched → verified |
  outcome_unknown`; only an undispatched `claimed` call may finish `failed`.
  Handler start is atomic and duplicate/terminal calls never enter the app
  handler.
- The race semantics are intentional: concurrent claimers can share a claim
  token, so a worker that loses before its callback must leave the claim
  unfinalized/reclaimable. It may not overwrite a row another worker advanced
  to `dispatched`. Any failure after confirmed handler start seals only
  `outcome_unknown`. Verified proof remains truthful even if the final database
  acknowledgement is unavailable, but the exact call remains replay-blocked
  and surfaces a warning.
- SwanBot v2 and OpenSwan now converge in source on the same runtime, approval,
  call identity, and sanitized hidden-receipt paths for these canaries. The
  default edge continuation lane also unions hard constraints parsed from the
  raw user turn with richer upstream constraints. Every client call passes an
  executable hard-floor/constraint check before an optional live exact-call
  review callback; a missing required gate, rejection, or callback exception
  fails closed before handler entry. When a review callback exists, client
  calls execute serially so concurrent prompts cannot reorder exact decisions.
  A non-empty always-confirm floor applies to every non-read browser/desktop
  call, including bland keypresses and future/unknown mutation names; a model
  cannot evade it by choosing an action whose arguments omit the floor keyword.
- Every browser/desktop mutation currently registered as a SwanBot v2
  client-delegated tool is intercepted before the raw dispatchers and enters
  `executeOpenSwanRuntimeTool` with the provider name/id/iteration. This closes
  the raw-bridge route for the current catalog, but it is not proof of a
  universal gateway for future tools, other callers, or mutation families that
  lack the five canaries' sealed proof and durable-ledger contract.
- The v2 edge `browser.fill_field` schema now matches the sealed client
  contract: one exact `name` xor `selector`, optional textbox/searchbox role,
  bounded non-secret text and task context, no submit field, no dropdown role,
  and no additional properties. The XOR rule is independently enforced by the
  edge model schema, app normalizer/sealed runtime, browser client request
  builders, and bridge target/perform endpoints; both-present and neither-
  present inputs fail before observation or dispatch. Dropdown work uses
  `browser.select_option`; credentials use the dedicated credential lane.
- Continuation snapshots now carry an opaque identity, protocol version, and
  one-time nonce. Before any local tool handler runs, the client-generated
  dispatch claim atomically changes `pending` / `client_pending` to
  `dispatch_claimed` / `client_dispatching`. Only the exact echoed claim may
  dispatch. Submitting its exact results then atomically rotates the snapshot
  to `results_claimed` / `client_resuming` before model resume; that result
  claim binds every later pending or terminal transition. Same-claim dispatch
  retries are idempotent, while a competing claim, ambiguous claim/result
  acknowledgement, expired lease, failed claim-bound transition, or post-claim
  loop error becomes `outcome_unknown` and is never reopened or automatically
  replayed. Readiness excludes active `client_pending`, `client_dispatching`,
  and `client_resuming` rows because none is terminal.
- Legacy `computerUse.ts` no longer executes `navigate`, `click`, `fill`,
  `select`, `press_key`, or `scroll` through raw browser, Stagehand, MCP, or
  bridge mutation APIs. Planner output, saved-plan hydration, session records,
  and execution rebuild a visible structured, non-executable handoff naming the
  corresponding typed OpenSwan tool while stripping raw mutation values. A
  fresh authenticated user/circle/persisted run/provider call/iteration/exact
  approval context is required before execution.
- `/replay` preflights the complete saved plan before step one. A plan
  containing any browser/desktop mutation executes zero steps and returns a
  typed non-executable OpenSwan handoff; only the reviewed observation allowlist
  may replay locally. A recording is planning evidence, never side-effect
  authority or a source of fabricated run/tool-use/approval identity.
- Catalog parity is pinned at **25 server-side + 59 client-delegated = 84
  total**. `browser.wait_for` and `browser.scroll` provide bounded semantic
  synchronization and viewport control through the canonical client runtime.
  Scroll dispatches one bounded wheel gesture only, captures before/after
  viewport position inside the bridge, and uses no more than three read-only
  settle samples to require requested-axis `movementVerified:true`. No movement
  or a viewport boundary returns `browser_scroll_verification_failed`; recovery
  collects a fresh DOM snapshot or screenshot before deciding whether another
  bounded gesture is safe, never blind replay. Raw coordinates, deltas, and
  viewport geometry are not returned.
  `browser.locator_actionability` is read-only advisory evidence for
  one fresh exact target, not mutation authorization or target binding.
  `browser.dom_snapshot` now redacts every editable value and excludes hidden,
  inert, and executable-source descendant text inside the bridge walker;
  canonicalizes bounded roles; exposes only controlled kind/state/length
  structure; and binds tree/title to one coherent entry/capture/exit document
  identity. The model receives only an HTTP(S) origin. Exact URL/document drift
  checks use a process-scoped HMAC URL identity plus opaque
  process/context/page identity; the HMAC rotates on bridge restart and all
  raw/forged legacy URL identities or non-HTTP snapshots fail closed.
  A dispatched client mutation whose result is failed, unverified, or
  outcome-unknown now latches that no-replay terminal integrity across
  continuations; model `end_turn` cannot convert it to completion. Fresh
  terminal compare-and-set/reread ambiguity stops before Feed publication,
  late cancellation wins, and the client propagates neutral cancellation
  without circuit-breaker accounting or v1 fallback. Empty reached-edge
  terminals are also non-fallback; direct WordPress/workspace mutations now
  issue exact-boundary value-free receipts and require concrete completion
  proof; and immutable `turnRequestId` metadata survives every fresh state
  replacement so lost-response retries collide before model/Feed replay. Focused smoke:
  `smoke:swanbot-v2-terminal-integrity`.
  Source/typecheck and focused contract/gateway smokes—including
  `smoke:computer-use-mutation-handoff` and the whole-plan recording
  guard—cover this slice, but the current edge source has not been
  deployed/re-verified.
  Continuations saved by the pre-deployment shape lack the required
  identity/version/nonce and intentionally fail closed instead of being
  resumed. The §26 migration is applied and catalog-verified on the target
  project; its real two-worker contention/crash-boundary behavior is still
  unproven, and no live browser/native-app GUI execution was performed. Eleven generic native
  input actions now have guarded one-attempt dispatch, but
  acknowledgement-only results explicitly report incomplete/unknown and seal
  `outcome_unknown`. Exact accessibility-value target sealing is now a separate
  source/contract-verified lane (see the 2026-08-05 status below); submit,
  upload, browser navigation/close, generalized native after-state
  verification, future mutation families, non-typed callers, and a complete
  universal gateway remain pending.

#### 2026-08-05 unfamiliar-app semantic workflow and native value sealing

- `buildGenericAppSemanticWorkflow` preserves the exact user utterance and
  produces a bounded schema-v1 semantic program rather than a cosmetic list of
  linguistic asks. The last checkpoint verifies every clause. Allowed control
  surfaces are adapters, lifecycle, app-native APIs/scripts, documented file
  adapters, embedded DOM/CDP, accessibility, semantic menus, and verified
  shortcuts; coordinates cannot appear in this workflow.
- The chat router now treats strongly framed `Use`/`Open`/`In <App>` requests,
  including long-tail app names, as desktop constraints and strips browser
  tools/fallbacks from that route. Literal URLs, named browser/web-only apps,
  WordPress, and transactional web intent remain browser-owned; Finder,
  Preview, and TextEdit file-shaped work remains local-file-owned. The exact
  Photoshop compiler remains an isolated closed-world branch.
- Approval classes are explicit. Reads/observations and exact launch/focus/wait
  create no redundant mutation prompt. Launch/focus still fail before action
  without authenticated persisted run/provider-call identity and accept only
  fresh exact-app proof. Reversible non-secret field/menu/toggle checkpoints
  share one bounded workflow review, while each runtime mutation keeps its
  exact receipt. Persistent/external/destructive/credential/payment/permission
  work and ambiguous choices keep an exact approval or user-choice floor.
- `desktop.set_element_value` now leaves the generic acknowledgement-only
  input lane. One fresh full accessibility observation binds exact
  app/PID/generation/dotted path/role/label plus current/requested value hashes
  and lengths into a short-lived one-shot non-secret target. A genuine
  exact-call approval receipt precedes one AX set-value dispatch. Completion
  requires a newer same-field observation with the requested hash and length;
  raw values and paths stay transient. Secure, credential, payment, permission,
  destructive, modal, stale, drifting, paste, coordinate, and raw-dispatch
  variants fail closed.
- An attempted mutation without accepted proof is `outcome_unknown`. The exact
  call cannot replay, later model prose cannot convert it to completion, and
  recovery is read-only verification before any new action.
- Source validation is green for the focused generic navigator and semantic
  value smokes, the 160-request / 7,410-assertion universal source eval, app
  routing, launch/focus, approval/grounding/runtime smokes, app typecheck, and
  diff checks. This is not live arbitrary-app GUI proof, a deployed edge, a
  database-contention proof, or evidence that OpenSwan can already complete
  every human action in every app.

#### 2026-08-06 exact-program identity and manual verification hardening

- The exact Photoshop executor now persists one authenticated root `agent_runs`
  row before desktop access, binds the originating Chat message/submission plus
  exact executable manifest into its root/action identity, derives one compiler-
  scoped dispatch capability, and runs create through §26 claim -> start ->
  finish. Approval and capability re-entry preserve the same request identity;
  missing, legacy, or mismatched identity fails before root creation and desktop
  access. Duplicate execution for that request/action cannot re-enter the bridge
  and post-start ambiguity is `outcome_unknown`. A new explicit submission
  intentionally receives a distinct request/root; refresh/restart propagation
  and live two-worker contention still need proof.
- `manual_verify_only` recovery is source-bound to the original requester,
  current task, exact bridge process instance, and exact target. It permits only
  allowlisted observation tools, rechecks task/bridge scope after every await
  and before transcript/archive persistence, suppresses stale task affordances,
  and never marks the task complete. Photoshop document status inspects the
  active document without activating another document. Focused smokes cover
  these contracts; no live recovery or cross-device continuation is claimed.
- Foreground activation is a one-boundary lease, not continuous ownership. A
  requested target may be raised once; if the user then switches to Terminal or
  another app, that human override interrupts the task. Retries, observations,
  progress UI, and approval polling must not keep pulling the target or Chat
  browser back to the front.

#### 2026-08-06 atomic universal-root/§26 canary seam

- §34 now owns combined root/action claim, start, and settle RPCs. They lock the
  root first, derive the §26 identity from that locked root action including its
  canonical run id, and advance one §26 row per mutation action without a
  split-brain write. Settlement can atomically complete/fail the root; only the
  exact proof-bound reconciliation path may move matching root and §26
  `outcome_unknown` state to `verified`. Generic §26 remains unchanged.
- `computerTaskRootStore` exposes the combined gateway only from its branded
  database binding. Memory/clone inputs fail closed, and only exact `started`
  issues a one-shot handler authority. Claimed refresh recovery returns a live
  token without a root revision bump or rotates an expired token server-side;
  fresh post-lock clocks/token checks govern claim/start/settle. STOP and human
  override are rejected while they would strand a claimed action.
- The first runtime consumer is a triple-flagged, default-off Photoshop canary
  limited to an already-running, frontmost app and one create mutation. It uses
  root A's run id directly and never creates child wrapper B, launches, focuses,
  raises, or touches the browser. Exact app/PID/CGWindow/bounds are rechecked in
  the bridge immediately before JSX; baseline, correlation receipt, and final
  document id/count/dimensions must agree. Final foreground is telemetry only,
  excluded from the proof digest, never refocused, and cannot downgrade proof.
  Requirement fingerprints are realized in runtime bindings; persisted roots
  are fenced before generic fallback and stale same-action leases rebind only
  after fresh exact observation. A post-start ambiguity cannot replay.
- This source slice ran no live Photoshop mutation, production §34 application,
  or deployment. The sole call wrapper now consumes exact in-process
  binding/action/tool/args/target/revision authority immediately before the
  bridge call, but the bridge cannot verify that authority. P0 blockers are
  bridge-verifiable attestation and durable STOP intent plus atomic claimed
  cancellation. A durable receipt for later `outcome_unknown` reconciliation
  is P1. Production migration/live canary, behavioral crash cuts, contention/
  recovery, and broader lifecycle branches remain blocked.

#### 2026-06-02 SwanBot/OpenSwan worktree configuration guard

- `openswanWorktreeConfig` and `openswan-worktree-config-report` now audit the local agent/worktree configuration
  before SwanBot/OpenSwan or connected agents take work: root guidance files,
  required package scripts, OpenSwan worktree-local stale-doc deferral,
  generated-artifact ignore rules, and noisy git-status lines. It formats a
  compact prompt block so the chat/runtime can keep worktree blockers hidden
  until an agent handoff needs them.
- Smoke coverage: `npm run smoke:openswan-worktree-config` validates synthetic
  blocker/watch cases and the live repo configuration. `npm run
  check:openswan-worktree-config` is the operator-facing live preflight and
  `smoke:all` runs the smoke next to bridge and Office roster/readiness checks.
- Managed Claude Code, Codex, Cursor Composer, and Gemini CLI bridge launches
  now append the hidden SwanBot/OpenSwan worktree config block through
  `terminal-launch-utils`, but only when the target `projectDir` looks like the
  Underground Circle/OpenSwan repo. Launches never fail solely because the
  config report cannot be generated.

#### 2026-05-18 desktop file-write bridge expansion

- Local file runtime now covers scoped search/read plus write primitives:
  rename/move, bounded UTF-8 text write/append, copy, create folder, and
  move-to-Trash. All write paths require the one-time write-scoped local file
  session grant and route through `scripts/claude-bridge.js`,
  `desktopBridge.ts`, `computerFileAdapter.ts`, and `openswanToolRuntime.ts`.
- Chat/local-intent routing sends explicit file mutations through the file task
  adapter instead of generic Browserbase/computer-use. Delete language maps to
  move-to-Trash; permanent delete remains intentionally absent.

#### 2026-05-21 runtime approval and recovery hardening

- Privileged OpenSwan browser/desktop/app tools now use stable tool+args
  approval fingerprints. Pending approvals defer duplicate calls, approved
  matching approvals pass the exact tool call, rejected approvals fail closed,
  expired approvals request a fresh approval, and legacy payloads remain
  pending-only for compatibility.
- Connected-agent failure recovery now launches Codex only when the recovery
  policy allows auto-fix and no user action is required. CAPTCHA, MFA,
  missing permission, and auth-style blockers return a blocked recovery result
  instead of starting an unattended app-update task.
- Smoke coverage: `smoke:openswan-runtime-approval` locks exact approval
  matching, and `smoke:agent-failure-recovery` locks the protected no-launch
  recovery path.
- Main chat failure branches now update the visible pending assistant message
  with the raw failure plus a bounded recovery handoff instead of dropping the
  error into archive only. The shared `chatFailureRecovery` helper covers
  OpenSwan batch failures, outer chat execution failures, and browser
  computer-use agent errors while preserving the no-launch guard for CAPTCHA,
  MFA, auth, and permission blockers.
- Early chat command failures now use the same recovery path for terminal-agent
  control/launch, multi-agent dispatch wrappers, `/v2`, memory bank, recording,
  desktop diagnostics, automation commands, local desktop bridge reads, local
  browser-plan execution, and agent plan mode. This keeps command-layer
  exceptions visible, persisted, archived, and recoverable instead of isolated
  one-line local errors.
- The same command-layer handoff now covers bridge probe exceptions, `/assign`
  dispatch failures, memory-service exceptions, scheduled-action exceptions,
  mission/summary/room exceptions, HF/GitHub/WordPress/Vault command exceptions,
  web-search fallback exhaustion, desktop-bridge pairing exceptions, and assign
  panel OpenSwan/task dispatch failures. Normal usage/validation messages remain
  direct local replies; only actual exceptions enter recovery.
- Chat failure recovery now fingerprints each failed task/error/source and keeps
  a short in-memory repeat ledger. Recent matching failures still show and
  archive the diagnosis, but duplicate connected-agent handoffs are suppressed
  so a broken chat route cannot launch or spam recovery sessions in a loop.
- Duplicate suppression is success-aware: a repeat is suppressed only after a
  previous matching failure actually reached a connected recovery handoff. If
  the first handoff failed, the next matching failure may try again. Recovery
  prompts now include route intent, complexity, chat mode, session profile,
  resolved model, web-search state, and connected-provider context.
- Recovery handoffs now include a source-specific verification plan. OpenSwan,
  desktop/bridge, browser/computer-use, provider/web-search, memory, schedule,
  and mission/agent failures each add the narrow smoke commands and checks the
  recovery agent should run, while archive metadata stores the suggested
  verification commands for later audit.
- Failure recovery policies now also emit a machine-readable runbook with next
  actor, bounded steps, retry limit, and stop conditions. Chat archives persist
  the runbook and OpenSwan `agent.recover_failed_task` returns it so future UI
  and planner loops can render or execute the safe next step instead of parsing
  free-form recovery text.
- Model/tool mismatch, rate-limit, and provider-unavailable failures now route
  to `switch_route_or_model` with a safe-route runbook instead of being treated
  as app-code patches or blind grounded retries.
- Complex failed tasks now get a recovery complexity assessment. Cross-surface
  browser/desktop/provider/memory/schedule/mission failures emit
  `decompose_then_recover` runbooks with surface mapping, subtask
  decomposition, checkpoint steps, expanded per-subtask verification, and a
  `CHECKPOINTS` output requirement before further retries or patches.

#### 2026-04-21 optimization pass (Chat / SwanBot / OpenSwan)

Reliability and performance fixes identified by per-subsystem audit. All shipped
this session; `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` clean.

- **OpenSwan auth-latch → time-expiring cache** (`src/lib/openswanService.ts`).
  `authFailedEndpointCache` converted from permanent `Set` to a
  `Map<string, expiryMs>` with a 30 s cooldown via new `isAuthFailed()` helper.
  One stale token at page load no longer disables Office for the rest of the
  session; the next success transparently clears the cache entry.
- **OpenSwan proxy — WebSocket auth injection**
  (`openswan-proxy.js`). The WS upgrade now overwrites `Authorization` with
  the locally-loaded gateway token, matching the HTTP path. Fixes silent WS
  403s from stale client tokens.
- **SwanBot prompt cache — timestamp removed from frozen prefix**
  (`supabase/functions/swanbot-ai/index.ts:451`). Soul-wisdom section no
  longer inlines `wisdom.generated_at`, so the Anthropic prompt cache
  actually survives across days instead of invalidating nightly.
- **SwanBot silent failures logged** (`swanbot-ai/index.ts:1897, 2865, 2875`).
  `logClaudeUsage`, `storeKnowledgeEntry`, and `extractAndStoreMemories`
  `.catch(()=>{})` replaced with `.catch(err => console.warn(...))`. Memory
  data-loss and usage-metering drift are now visible in the edge-function
  logs.
- **SwanBot volatile prompt 4000-char cap**
  (`swanbot-ai/index.ts:596`). Tail-truncation with warn log when the
  volatile block exceeds 4 kB — priority-ordered sections mean low-value
  wiki content is dropped first, not critical circle info.
- **Chat realtime N+1 → O(1)** (`ChatTab.tsx:2558`). Realtime INSERT handler
  now resolves sender display name from a `membersRef` cache populated at
  mount. Fallback `.from('profiles').select(...)` preserved for users who
  joined mid-session.
- **Chat per-row `ErrorBoundary`** (`ChatTab.tsx:5622` + style
  `messageRowFallback`). One malformed message can no longer crash the
  whole tab; compact red inline chip replaces it with "Couldn't render this
  message."
- **Chat `MessageRow` `React.memo` + custom comparator**
  (`ChatTab.tsx:7378–7617`). Typing or receiving a message no longer
  cascades renders across every row. Comparator ignores callback identity
  (inline closures), compares value props.
- **Chat `FlatList` virtualization** (`ChatTab.tsx:6070`).
  `initialNumToRender=15`, `maxToRenderPerBatch=10`, `windowSize=10`,
  `updateCellsBatchingPeriod=50`, `removeClippedSubviews` (native only).
  `getItemLayout` deliberately skipped — variable row heights make a
  guessed estimate cause scroll jumps.
- **Chat lazy-init — first paint on 20 messages**
  (`ChatTab.tsx:2342`). Initial `loadThreadMessages` page size dropped from
  50 → 20; 150 ms after paint the existing `handleLoadOlder` quietly
  fetches the next batch. Time-to-first-paint cut by ~500 ms on longer
  threads.
- **`src/lib/devLog.ts`** added — tiny opt-in trace logger. Three
  highest-frequency `console.log` sites
  (`agentRunSystem.ts:613`, `agentAutoConnect.ts:571`,
  `agentSessionMemory.ts:254, 312`) converted to `devLog.trace(...)` so
  DevTools isn't spammed by per-event chatter. Enable with
  `localStorage.UC_DEBUG = 'trace'` + reload.
- **Edge-function config**: `[functions.llm-proxy]` with
  `verify_jwt = false` added to `supabase/config.toml` and redeployed —
  fixes the 401 that silently disabled `memoryEmbeddings`.
- **Deploys this session**: `llm-proxy` (verify_jwt fix), `swanbot-ai`
  (all five SwanBot changes above).
- **Chat realtime fallback polling** (`ChatTab.tsx:2528`
  + `src/lib/chatService.ts loadNewerThreadMessages`). Supabase channel
  `.subscribe(status => ...)` observes status events; any non-`SUBSCRIBED`
  status (CHANNEL_ERROR / TIMED_OUT / CLOSED) starts a 15 s poll loop with
  an immediate first fetch. The incoming-row handler was extracted into
  `mergeIncomingRow` so realtime and polling paths share identical dedup /
  optimistic-match / name-resolution logic. Poll stops on recovery.
  Eliminates the silent "users miss messages when realtime drops" failure
  mode.
- **Stale plan docs archived** — `docs/archive/` now holds
  `LAUNCH_AUDIT_2026-02-24.md`, `OFFICE_DASHBOARD_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-04-03.md`,
  `OPENSWAN_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT_2026-04-15.md`, and two pre-2026-03-06
  `supabase-migration*.sql` files. `docs/archive/README.md` documents
  supersession. Agents picking up work won't re-read superseded context.

Protocol v2 binds exact identity/version/nonce plus a client-generated dispatch claim: `pending` / `client_pending` becomes `dispatch_claimed` / `client_dispatching` before any local tool executes, then the exact result set becomes `results_claimed` / `client_resuming` before the model loop resumes. The exact messages, raw tool arguments, and result payloads exist only transiently or inside an AES-256-GCM envelope produced by `supabase/functions/_shared/swanbot-continuation-crypto.ts`; its additional authenticated data binds canonical exact run/user/circle row identity, while public checkpoint fields contain bounded identities, counts, state, timestamps, and tool names only and reveal none of those row-binding identifiers. Cross-row ciphertext transplantation therefore fails closed. The edge requires dedicated `SWANBOT_CONTINUATION_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` key material, withholds client-only tools when it is unavailable, and will not resume an unreadable or legacy checkpoint. Migration §29 validates public envelopes, closes unsafe/expired active checkpoints without replay, repairs swept rows to array/integer-safe run-summary shapes without copying values into outcome metadata, and scrubs all terminal checkpoints. Only an exact retry of the already-winning dispatch claim is acknowledged idempotently. Competing claims, mixed deployments/states, ambiguous claim or result acknowledgements, expired dispatch/resume leases, resume-loop failure, or lost next-pending/terminal writes become non-retryable `outcome_unknown`; neither claimed state is reopened and client actions are never automatically replayed. Older pre-deployment pending snapshots lack the protocol-v2 identity contract and intentionally fail closed. ### Phase 1d — Edge-function Anthropic consolidation (near-complete)

Audit on 2026-04-21 found 7 edge functions that call Anthropic directly without using the central pricing/cache/telemetry helpers. Worst drift: `chat-stream` was computing cost as `(input * 0.8 + output * 4.0) / 1M` (not a valid rate for any Claude model) and always writing `cache_creation_tokens: 0, cache_read_tokens: 0`.

Migration rule: every edge function that calls Anthropic routes through `supabase/functions/_claude/anthropic.ts` for pricing, cache accounting, and `claude_api_usage` logging.

### Per-circle budget caps (Rule #12 rollout)

| Feature | Setting key | Default | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Computer Use | `circles.settings.computer_use_max_cost_usd` | $2.00 per run | Cumulative cost check before every Claude turn; aborts run with SSE error when exceeded |
| Automations | `circles.settings.automation_max_cost_usd` | $1.00 per rolling 24h | Sum of `claude_api_usage` where source='automation-executor' within last 24h; skips run with logStep message when exceeded |

Both configurable from `CircleSettingsScreen` (COMPUTER USE BUDGET + AUTOMATION DAILY CAP sections, with preset chips). Matches Rule #12 — "Budget caps are a per-circle setting, not a code constant."

---

## 4. Execution-loop convergence

The in-app parent and child migrations are complete. Current topology:

| Path | Entry | Loop | Rollout state |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenSwan session | `src/lib/openswanSessionRuntime.ts` | `agentExecutionCore.runAgent` + `src/lib/openswanBridge.ts` | Default on; explicit next-turn legacy rollback only |
| OpenSwan subagent | `src/lib/subagentRegistry.ts` | `agentExecutionCore.runAgent` with isolated child context | Default on; explicit next-delegation legacy rollback only |
| SwanBot batch client canary | `src/lib/swanbotV2BatchRuntime.ts` | `agentExecutionCore.runAgent` + canonical OpenSwan tools | Device-local opt-in/default off |
| SwanBot v2 edge | `supabase/functions/swanbot-v2-ai/index.ts` | Edge tool loop + single-consumer validated client continuations | Source-default path; current source is not deployed/re-verified |
| SwanBot v1 edge | `supabase/functions/swanbot-ai/index.ts` | Legacy in-process tool loop | Retained until M4 telemetry and M5 deletion gates close |

Source convergence now covers the complete **84-tool** SwanBot v2 catalog
(**25 server-side + 59 client-delegated**), including bounded semantic
`browser.wait_for` / `browser.scroll` bound before and after execution to one
fresh opaque process/context/page/process-HMAC-URL identity, read-only advisory
`browser.locator_actionability`, the three sealed browser canaries, and narrow
native semantic press through the canonical OpenSwan runtime. Locator
actionability does not authorize or bind a later mutation. The default edge
path now merges raw-turn/upstream constraints, runs
the executable hard floor and exact live review callback before dispatch,
serializes approval prompts, and routes every currently registered
client-delegated browser/desktop mutation through that runtime rather than a
raw bridge. Continuations use two claim-bound CAS phases:
`client_pending → client_dispatching` before any local side effect, then
`client_dispatching → client_resuming` before model resume. Ambiguity seals
outcome-unknown/no-replay. Readiness ignores all three active states.
Wait/scroll source rejects tab, navigation, reload, SPA-URL, close, context, and
bridge drift; element waits use exact ARIA role/name semantics. Scroll emits one
bounded wheel gesture, takes at most three privacy-local read-only settle
samples, and requires requested-axis `movementVerified:true`; boundary/no-motion
failure returns `browser_scroll_verification_failed` for fresh DOM/screenshot
recovery without blind replay, and exposes no viewport geometry. That is not a
universal-gateway or live browser claim:
future tools/callers still require explicit coverage and the device-local typed
client remains default-off. The
2026-08-05 production report live-verified deployed v1/v2 function presence,
JWT modes, production-origin CORS, required secret names, and the Chat catalog
contract; it did not prove that deployed v2 code matches the newer 84-tool
source snapshot. A
600x600 Photoshop scratch-document run also proved the exact native path and
stable foreground focus. §29, fresh clean v1/v2 telemetry, live continuation
claim/key-rotation proof, arbitrary native semantic input, and Postgres
contention remain unproven. Pre-deployment continuation snapshots fail closed
because they lack the new identity contract.

The separate hosted Browserbase Computer Use lane is now source-hardened as of
2026-07-26. Before provider or session work, its edge requires a bounded
schema-v1 policy for authenticated Chat/queue callers; scheduled watch/service
calls are forced observation-only, while authenticated legacy callers without
the policy now receive HTTP 400. Root Chat preserves derived user constraints
and confirmation floors on all three cloud starts: automatic browser launch,
booking-session continuation, and manual approved launch. Every native
left/right/double click, type, key, and saved-login mutation requires durable
exact-call live confirmation, then a fresh pre-action screenshot, one-attempt
dispatch, and a fresh post-action screenshot. Unknown mutations fail closed;
ambiguous post-dispatch state becomes `mutation_outcome_unknown` and is never
automatically replayed. Typed/key/credential/question inputs are redacted or
withheld from SSE/action/progress traces, model history, guided replay, stuck
solver payloads, usage metadata, and error strings. The single-task and queue
hooks synchronously reserve starts before async imports/credential lookup and
invalidate pending reservations on cancellation.

Root Chat's non-browser runtime also removed its generic pre-agent mutation
bypasses. `computerTaskRuntime` no longer calls the deterministic app adapter
or bridge open/wait helpers for model-planned work before authenticated
`executeAgentRun`; read-only live observation remains. The one narrow exception
is a compiler-owned exact program whose full calls, arguments, and narrow
authorization mode are already known before dispatch. The first family creates a blank
Photoshop document through status, conditional launch, exact create, and final
status proof without the AI relay. Its executor persists an authenticated root
run before desktop access and binds create to §26 under the exact compiler
manifest. A bounded unsaved draft uses the current
direct request; an oversized allocation retains a one-shot Chat plan approval.
Unverified post-dispatch outcomes cannot
replay. All other app/hybrid work reaches the typed agent loop. Uploaded files
are persisted and exact-row linked before any consumer, then enter the sealed
attachment-source contract. The legacy signed-URL/path-bearing desktop branch
is disabled, and its compatibility descriptor carries no local path, stage
directory, storage path, signed URL, or executable authority. Content reads
use `attachments.read_source`. One explicit open/load/preview/show request for
one safe durably linked upload may use the separate one-shot
`desktop.open_attachment({ attachmentId })` path. Its bytes, bearer, local path,
exact message scope, and approval-resume lease remain process-private; approval
is non-batchable, bridge dispatch is single-use, and only a runtime-issued
receipt plus a fresh proof of the privately resolved app, frontmost process and
window, and exact unpredictable native front-document identity can complete A1.
Arbitrary accessibility text, window titles, error dialogs, and launch
acknowledgements are never completion proof. This boundary is covered by
`desktop-attachment-app-identity-smoketest.ts` alongside the capability and
runtime smokes. Crash leftovers and
idle expirations are removed by guarded bridge cleanup. Edit/save/export/
transform requests, copied or expired authority, scope/hash drift, missing
proof, and uncertain post-dispatch state remain blocked or `outcome_unknown`
without replay; this tool never falls through to `desktop.open_path`.

The second compiler family is strict named-app lifecycle. It fingerprints the
originating Chat request, persists or recovers the authenticated root, and uses
one request-only §26 activation key before desktop mutation. The key remains
stable when a long-tail app name becomes canonical after launch; the exact
program fingerprint remains separate, so such drift recovers the prior owner
and fails closed rather than creating another activation. `open_app` chooses
only one branch from fresh initial state: launch plus bounded wait when stopped,
or focus when already running/background. It never launches and then focuses;
an explicit focus request never launches. A terminal action returned after a
refresh cannot enter either branch again. This is source/focused-smoke coverage;
no updated live lifecycle GUI, duplicate-refresh, foreground-override,
bridge-restart, or competing-client test has been run.

The separate compiler-generated exact Photoshop drill was run once after a
local bridge restart on 2026-08-06. It dispatched exactly one create, proved
`Untitled-4` at 600x600 with Photoshop frontmost, recorded zero browser calls,
and five subsequent foreground samples remained in Photoshop. This proves only
the closed-world direct compiler/bridge/app path; it is not canonical Chat
lifecycle, duplicate-refresh, competing-client, hosted-edge, or production-site
proof.

`computerTaskOutcome` now also contains the pure V1 outer task-acceptance core.
It can compile an immutable root/ordered-action/typed-predicate contract, issue
only the next action as an opaque WeakSet-branded single-use claim, seal
runtime-branded value-free action evidence only from that exact claim, evaluate
exact Photoshop dimensions or named-app frontmost state from fresh typed
observations, and issue one concurrency-reserved receipt only after final
evidence follows every action. Claim issuance and sealing reserve synchronously;
plain/JSON clones, copied binding strings, cross-contract/out-of-order/reused
claims, and concurrent double sealing fail closed.
Generic evidence summarization deliberately remains incapable of setting the
completion bit. No Chat, SwanBot, agent-runtime, compiler, recovery, or
persistence caller is wired to this issuer yet, and its in-process identity does
not survive a reload. It is source/focused-test groundwork, not deployed or live
task-completion proof.

Live validation on 2026-07-31 submitted the motivating sentence through the
authenticated refreshed Chat UI from a fresh `appRunning:false` Photoshop
status. Chat created no new `agent_approvals` row, persisted one completion,
and the final app-native status reported `Untitled-1`, one active document,
600x600 px, RGB, 72 ppi, and one layer. The older approval transcript remains
historical; its expired Needs-you item is suppressed once the newer verified
computer-task completion is visible.

The 2026-07-26 authority consolidation also closes the remaining audited
approval and unattended-dispatch gaps. Chat consumes one exact plan/scope
digest through `agent_approvals.applied_at`; OpenSwan consumes one exact
tool/scope dispatch binding; and SwanBot WordPress writes plus the generic
risk floor use the same schema-v2 digest and one-use claim semantics. Raw
commands, paths, values, credentials, and canonical argument keys stay out of
durable approval and model-visible payloads. Legacy direct local-file,
image-conversion, and diagnostic launch helpers return only value-free,
non-executable typed-tool handoffs. The executable `desktop.open_path` lane
instead requires fresh stat/path digests, authenticated run/provider-call
identity, exact approval, §26 claim/start, one bridge attempt, and exact
frontmost-app proof.

Manual automation file writes and every scheduled external action now have an
irreversible dispatch boundary. Service/scheduled automation invocation stays
read-only; approved manual writes and scheduled occurrences use fresh
single-use authority, one dispatch, verification, and persistent
`outcome_unknown` on ambiguity. The Pending Actions UI exposes a redacted
verify-first state and no retry for ambiguity. Office Realtime commands are
only wakeups: receivers authenticate and reread the exact circle-scoped
durable row before reconstructing the command. Approval-gated custom API and
messaging edge writes also require §26 ledger authority. Section 26 is applied
and catalog-verified, but its real concurrent-worker behavior remains
unproven. Sections 27 and 28 remain unapplied, so their scheduled-action and
database-approval/Office guarantees remain source/focused-smoke claims—not
live database, deployment, concurrent-worker, or GUI proof.

These are source and focused-smoke claims. The updated
`computer-use-agent` edge has not been deployed/re-verified; no live
Browserbase run, Supabase confirmation-row integration, or native-app GUI run
was performed. The new HTTP 400 for authenticated legacy Computer Use callers
without a v1 policy is an intentional compatibility boundary.
`smoke:computer-use-cloud-policy` and
`smoke:computer-task-runtime-context` are canonical readiness requirements and
run exactly once in Chat daily, Chat release, and `smoke:all`;
`smoke:chat-computer-request-router` guards the root Chat derivation.
`smoke:browser-wait-scroll-reachability` is also a canonical readiness
requirement and runs in both release gates; it pins the bounded semantic wait/
scroll surface without adding raw selector or coordinate authority. It also
pins the complete opaque process/context/page/URL input, direct exact ARIA
role/name lookup, retained page reference, and pre/post identity checks that
reject tab/navigation/reload/SPA-URL/close/bridge drift.
`smoke:photoshop-exact-drill` likewise runs exactly once in the Chat daily and
release commands through their matching npm `precheck:*` lifecycle hooks, and
once in `smoke:all`; the canonical `check:*` bodies do not call those hooks
again. It proves the compiler-derived manifest and Chrome-free drill
guardrails. The separately required
`smoke:computer-task-runtime-context` and
`smoke:computer-sequence-program-core` pin the corresponding production source
contract, including origin-message/program-bound root reuse, fail-closed missing
identity, and the §26 boundary, but the drill pair is
not shared-helper or end-to-end execution parity.
`npm run drill:photoshop-exact` remains a dry run unless an
operator explicitly supplies `--live` and the fingerprint printed by that dry
run. One 2026-08-05 invocation dispatched one create and no browser calls; its
strict immediate status was stale, so it exited verification-incomplete, and a
separate fresh read-only status proved the active 600x600 result. This is not
authenticated Chat, approval-persistence, browser-event, or post-retry live-
create proof.
The exact approval, direct-handoff, open-path, automation, schedule, Office,
and database-authority guards are also mandatory exactly once in both
SwanBot/Chat daily and release chains and in `smoke:all`.
Both release gates now include `smoke:computer-task-truthful-outcome`; Chat
release additionally includes `smoke:chat-failure-recovery`. The React hook-
order regression behind the Chat thinking-label console error is pinned by
`smoke:thinking-label-hook-order` directly in the Chat release gate. Both
release gates also run `smoke:swanbot-v2-thread-identity`, which pins the active
authenticated Chat thread through fresh and resumed v2 requests and prevents a
model argument from choosing a service-role message-write target. Chat release
also runs `smoke:exact-program-authority` to pin the authenticated root and §26
claim/start/finish boundary around compiler-owned exact mutations.

`smoke:computer-task-truthful-outcome` now directly covers the pure outer-
acceptance compiler/evaluator/issuer, including root/contract/action binding,
fresh final-proof ordering, forgery/reorder/reuse rejection, concurrent issuance
reservation, and one-use receipt application. Because no production caller is
wired to that issuer, this remains source-core proof and generic task completion
stays inconclusive.

Chat local-file grants are demand-driven as of 2026-08-06: `ChatTab` no longer
requests `~/Desktop` write authority on mount. The only interactive grant path
runs after the compiled task declares `file_read` or `file_write`, scopes the
request to inferred task roots, and uses the exact task as its reason. The
`smoke:chat-file-permission-demand` source/AST guard is a canonical readiness
requirement and runs transitively in daily, release, and `smoke:all` through the
readiness gate. This is source proof, not a live browser permission-dialog test.

Browser credential schema parity is source-hardened as of 2026-08-06. The app
and SwanBot v2 edge require `credentialField` plus exactly one non-empty
circle-vault `credentialId` or 1Password `item`, reject unknown fields, and the
runtime repeats the XOR guard before approval and at handler entry. The
`smoke:browser-credential-schema-parity` regression runs through the existing
v2 edge-fill gate; no live credential fill was performed.

The next production gateway slice is deliberately ordered: add exhaustive
catalog `mutationAuthority` classification and fail closed on unclassified
writes; extract the shared §26 gateway into `agentActionCalls.ts`; then migrate
`browser.open_url` with separate warm-context and cold-context transition proof.
Cold navigation needs a paired process-only lifecycle capability and exactly
one context creation/navigation; `/browser/health` is observation UI, not
authority. Only after those receipts are genuine should the compiler-owned
Photoshop/lifecycle families feed the outer task-acceptance issuer. Durable
acceptance rehydration requires a new migration and authenticated CAS store;
post-hoc generic manifests and JSON-cloned receipts remain forbidden.

Remaining convergence work:

1. Carry genuine live thread, cancellation, and plugin context into every
   `runAgent` entry and audit new callers. Raw-turn plus upstream
   user-constraint/always-confirm context and the live exact-call review
   callback now reach both SwanBot v2 client implementations in source.
   Never fabricate an approval gate; policy that requires approval must fail
   closed when no real UI/operator gate is available.
2. Gather the production telemetry required by the SwanBot/OpenSwan readiness
   report before changing the client-loop default.
3. Keep the v2 edge as the rollback path through the canary window, then retire
   v1 only through the M5 checklist.
4. Move `extractBrowserPlansFromToolActions` to its canonical consumer and
   delete `openswanRuntimeToolLoop.ts` after the rollback window.
5. Finish compiler-owned exact identity. Photoshop blank-document creation now
   fingerprints the originating Chat message/submission plus program, reuses its
   authenticated root/action across approval or capability re-entry, and uses
   the applied `agent_action_calls` claim -> dispatch -> verify/outcome-unknown
   lifecycle. Missing or mismatched identity blocks before root/desktop access;
   a new explicit submission intentionally gets a new request/root. Next, prove
   cross-process restart and competing clients preserve the admitted request
   identity. The feature-off frontmost-only canary now eliminates child wrapper
   B, uses root A's canonical run id, projects its one create as one §26 row,
   and uses root-first combined claim/start/settle plus optional terminal root
   transition. Its exact proof reconciliation advances matching root and §26
   `outcome_unknown` state atomically; generic §26 remains immutable. The local
   sole-call wrapper now exact-consumes the started authority, but trusted
   bridge-verifiable attestation, durable STOP intent/atomic claimed
   cancellation, and a durable later-reconciliation receipt remain missing.
   Apply §34 only after those close, then pass a live production canary,
   two-worker contention, behavioral crash-cut/recovery, and foreground-
   override drills. Do not widen the Photoshop family before those gates close.
6. Make human foreground override a coordinator interrupt. Initial requested
   activation may focus the exact target once; after the user switches away,
   polling, progress UI, approval waits, and retries must pause/fail closed
   instead of repeatedly raising the app or Chat browser.
7. Wire the source-landed V1 outer-acceptance core into supported task families.
   Today it has only exact Photoshop-dimensions and named-app-frontmost
   predicates, runtime-issued in-process brands, and direct focused-test callers.
   Production must compile the admitted contract, seal real ordered action
   evidence, collect fresh final predicate evidence, persist/rehydrate a
   value-free receipt, and only then expose task-level completion.

Universal Computer Task Kernel rollout follows the phases in
`docs/UC_APP_TASK_RELIABILITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`: (0) identity/authority inventory,
(1) structured action-evidence transport, (2) finish durable root identity and
wire the source-landed outer task-acceptance core around the request-bound
Photoshop and named-app lifecycle §26 canaries, (3) a catalog-wide mutation
gateway, (4) server-CAS task
leases/checkpoints and durable approval continuation, (5) browser/native
surface breadth plus capability buildout, (6) one cross-surface task ledger,
and (7) fault-injection/live production gates. The policy matrix in that doc
is normative for new computer tools: observation, lifecycle, bounded local
draft, local/external mutation, publish/send, credential/payment, destructive,
coordinate fallback, and capability buildout may require different approval,
but none may bypass exact identity, target binding, dispatch truth, or proof.

`src/lib/agentTools/` was retired in full on 2026-06-10. Every unique
capability belongs in `openswanToolRuntime` and the shared adapter is
`src/lib/openswanBridge.ts`. Do not re-create a side registry.

Inverse hazard: `memory_entries.user_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `auth.users`, so deleting a user destroys the `circle_shared` memories they authored. §30 section 8 documents the proposed fix (`deactivated_at` column, service-only `purge_deactivated_memories()`, a real hard-delete option, and a split private/shared story for departing users) and explains why the "safe half" — clearing `embedding` on deactivate — was rejected rather than shipped. | **Open — product decision, not a migration** |

When in doubt, rerun the relevant idempotent section and then reload schema.

## 6. Rules for contributing agents

1. **Before adding a new `openswan*.ts` or `agent*.ts` file, check §2.** If a file already owns the concern, extend it.
2. **Tool catalog goes in `openswanToolRuntime.ts`.** Don't create separate per-tool files unless you also plan the migration in this doc.
3. **Loop goes through `agentExecutionCore.runAgent`.** Don't reinvent a `while` with Anthropic fetch calls. Use `createAnthropicProvider`.
4. **Memory writes + skill writes MUST go through HITL.** Never write to `circle_memory`, `user_memory`, or `circle_skills` from a tool handler without an approval step.
5. **Retrieved content is untrusted.** Wrap `session_search` / `searchCircleMemory` results in `<untrusted_quoted>…</untrusted_quoted>` before giving them back to the model.
6. **Don't delete the deprecated files in §2 yet** — they have live callers. Remove only after the migrations in Phase 1c and Phase 3 land.
7. **Update this doc when you ship a phase item.** Move it from "Planned" to "Shipped" with a date.
8. **Agent-runtime SQL must stay mirrored.** Add normal app schema changes as migrations under `supabase/migrations/`. If the SQL is part of this agent/runtime roadmap, also mirror it into `docs/RUN_THIS_SQL.sql` and update this checklist.
9. **Use `safeGetUser` / `safeGetSession`, not `supabase.auth.getUser()` directly.** See `src/lib/authSession.ts`.
10. **Skills follow the [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) SKILL.md format** — YAML frontmatter + `## When to use / ## Procedure / ## Pitfalls / ## Verification` sections. No custom fields.
11. **Edge functions call Anthropic through `supabase/functions/_claude/anthropic.ts`.** Use `callClaude()` for non-streaming requests, `computeCostUsd()` + `logClaudeUsage()` for streaming ones (import-only — streaming loop stays in the function). Never hand-roll `fetch("https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages", ...)` — that drops you out of central pricing, cache accounting, and telemetry. If you need a feature the helper doesn't expose, extend the helper rather than bypass it.
12. **Budget caps are a per-circle setting, not a code constant.** Long-running or cron-triggered agents (Computer Use, automation-executor) MUST read a USD cap from `circles.settings.<feature>_max_cost_usd` with a safe default. Enforce in the loop using `computeCostUsd()` so it matches billing.
13. **Provider additions are cross-surface changes.** Keep `llmProviders`, `circleIntegrations`, `serviceProfileSouls`, `crossProviderRouter`, `billingPriority`, `llm-proxy`, `swanbot-ai`, UI cards, provider registries, and DB lookup/index migrations in sync.
14. **Native computer-use execution needs a computer-use-capable model.** The Browserbase/Anthropic edge loop should stay on Sonnet-capable models; route other selected models through planning/validation or fall back before the native screenshot/action loop.
15. **Guarded mutations never bypass the durable call boundary.** For the shipped fill/toggle/select/native-semantic canaries, claim only after genuine authorization, record `dispatched` inside the sealed handler immediately before bridge entry, and never translate a post-dispatch error to `failed`. A pre-handler race loser leaves the shared claim reclaimable; an attempted unverified action becomes `outcome_unknown`.
16. **SwanBot v2 client mutations enter through OpenSwan.** Keep every client-delegated browser/desktop mutation in the canonical runtime interception set before any raw dispatcher. Adding a future tool or caller requires its own parity, policy, approval, and proof audit; current catalog coverage is not a universal guarantee.
17. **A claimed continuation is single-consumer and one-way.** Preserve the exact identity/version/nonce plus client dispatch-claim id, the pre-side-effect `client_pending → client_dispatching` CAS, the pre-model-resume `client_dispatching → client_resuming` CAS, and claim filters on every next-pending/terminal write. Never reopen or automatically retry client actions after claim ambiguity; seal `outcome_unknown` and require fresh evidence.
18. **An approval is exact, current, and single-use.** Bind the complete normalized intent plus authenticated scope with SHA-256, persist only safe digests, and atomically consume the authority before one dispatch. Never treat a prior status lookup, truncated description, broadcast payload, or model decision as reusable authority.
19. **Realtime is notification, not command authority.** A subscriber must authenticate and reread the exact RLS-protected durable row before acting; reconstruct executable fields only from that row, reject stale or mismatched state, and suppress duplicate/in-flight wakeups.
20. **Protected schema-v2 authority is immutable in the database.** Preserve §28's narrow trigger predicates, allowlisted payload validators, member-only pending resolution with server-stamped resolver, requester-only expiry/one-use consume, and no protected-row delete or legacy-to-v2 conversion. Do not widen those triggers onto unrelated legacy or scheduled approval rows.

---

## 7. Open questions for Chris

(Same as §9 of `AGENT_RUNTIME_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md`; repeating for discoverability.)

**Decisions recorded 2026-06-09 (Chris):**

1. **Default Phase 1c model — DECIDED: Sonnet 4.6 for the edge function + Opus 4.7 for the in-app gateway.** Quality-first. Edge turns may need a lower `maxIterations` than the in-app gateway given Sonnet vs Opus cost.
2. **Ship `swanbot-v2-ai` side-by-side or in-place — DECIDED: in-place rewrite.** Rewrite `swanbot-ai` as a thin wrapper over the shared core; removes the dual-loop sooner. (Higher risk — sequence behind a verified v2 + readiness snapshot, and keep a rollback path.)
3. **External naming — DECIDED: keep "OpenSwan".** No rename. `default::blackswan` agent id and internal naming stay; zero migration cost.
4. **Skill marketplace visibility.** Circle-private by default (current schema), or add a public `is_public` flag for a UC-wide marketplace later? *(still open)*

---

## 8. How to keep this doc true

- Every time a phase item changes status, update §3 with the date.
- Every time a file moves between "Canonical" and "Deprecated", update §2.
- Every time SQL is applied, update §5.
- When Phase 1c lands, collapse §4 into a historical note.
- When Phase 5 preconditions are met, promote Phase 5 from "Gated" to "Planned".

If you find this doc lying, fix it in the same PR as the code change.
